ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u4	Mr. Marshal! Ministers! Mr. President of the Supreme Chamber of Control! Dear guests! High Seym! I have the honour to present to the High House a report by the Committee on Public Finance on the report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2018 together with a proposal for discharge to the Council of Ministers for that period. The state budget for 2018 has been the best budget of the state over the last several years, prepared by the finance minister Teresa Czerwińska under the direction of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki. It is a great satisfaction to present a report by the Committee on Public Finance on this very well-designed and implemented budget, the beneficiaries of which were all Poles. This budget provided Poland with further economic development and, at the same time, by financing important social programmes, it met the expectations and needs of Poles. So it was inclusive, or universal, and its positive effects were felt by millions of Poles. Budgets 2018 and 2019 are budgets of conscience, budgets with a soul, sensitive to the needs of Poles. The economic distinction between the 2018 budget was the lowest state budget deficit in the history of several recent years, which decreased from 1,3%. GDP in 2017 to the lowest: 0,5% GDP in 2018 For the first time in history, the state budget has approached the level of the sustainable budget after the transformation. The government could achieve this objective, but in order to meet the important social objectives of the Act of 6 December 2018 amending the Law on Specific Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2018, it provided an excess of funds for the additional expenditure set out in the Act. According to the EU methodology, the public finance deficit decreased from 1.5% GDP up to 0.4% GDP in 2018. Public debt calculated according to this methodology decreased from 50.6% GDP up to 48.9% GDP. It is worth mentioning that the sector's deficit in 2010 was 5.9% GDP and public debt amounted to 55% GDP. The general government deficit in 2010 amounted to more than PLN 111 billion and the general government deficit was about PLN 84 billion, compared to 10.4 billion in 2018. Currently, the sustainability of Polish public finances has been greatly improved and Poland meets the fiscal criteria of Economic and Monetary Union with a significant margin of security. The second important growth factor was GDP growth at constant prices. It reached 5.1% versus planned 3.8%. It was at the same time 3.1 percentage points higher than in the European Union, where it amounted to 2% at that time. It was also 1.5 percentage points higher than global GDP growth of 3.6%. In Poland in 2018 there was a very good economic situation on the domestic market. The main cause of this situation has been the achievement of almost zero unemployment in recent years. Although the registered unemployment rate remained at 5.8% , this unemployment measurement according to the BAEL survey shows its decline below 3.8%. This led to a significant acceleration of nominal wage growth, which amounted to 7% in 2018. The rapid increase in wages has led to an improvement in the social security system, reducing the need for funding from the state budget. According to analysts, we have not dealt in Poland in a decade with such a rapid increase in real wages. The rise in wages was largely accompanied by a clear increase in social benefits due to government policies. It should be stressed that the increase in Poles' income was mainly due to the increase in income from work and capital rather than to the increase in social benefits. The rapid increase in income resulted in an increase in individual consumption of 4.4%. which was accompanied by an even stronger increase in collective consumption. It should be stressed that the increase in consumption was accompanied by a very desirable acceleration in investment - an increase of almost 9% , partly due to an increased inflow of EU funds and thus to a sharp increase in the scale of public investment. The ratio of investments to gross domestic product increased to 18.2% and this is another step towards achieving the goal set out in the plan of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki at 25% GDP. GDP growth was rapid in the absence of signs of imbalances. Despite high wage dynamics, inflation did not exceed 2%, which kept interest rates unchanged. Another positive feature of the 2018 budget is the high increase in revenue. The realised revenues amounted to PLN 380 billion and were higher than the budget law by more than PLN 24 billion, which is close to 7%. Compared to revenue in 2017, there was an increase of PLN 30 billion, i.e. 8.5%. The tax revenue was even higher than 2017 as it reached close to 11%. In particular, the effects of the measures taken by the National Tax Administration sealing the tax system and a better macro-economic situation than originally planned have been affected by the clear increase in tax revenues. At this point, I would like to thank the first head of the National Tax Administration, the current finance minister, Mr Marian Banasi, for the consistency and determination in the implementation of the new tax administration, whose effects are tangible. [[Applause]] Non-tax revenue representing 7,6% the total revenue exceeded the forecast set out in the Budget Act by nearly PLN 7 billion, i.e. 32%. In 2018, VAT revenues amounted to PLN 175 billion and were nearly 9 billion higher than the plan. Compared to 2017, VAT revenues increased by more than 11% , exactly 11.6%. The excise tax revenue amounted to more than 72 billion and amounted to 103% a plan from the budget law. Compared to 2017, excise income increased by 5.6%. In 2018, the planned revenues from the gaming tax were 16% higher than in 2017. The increase in these revenues should be linked to amendments to the Law of December 2016 on gambling. The income from personal income tax, i.e. from PIT, amounted to nearly PLN 60 billion in 2018, and was 7.3% higher than the plan and 13% higher than the year 2017. At the same time, income from corporate income tax, i.e. CIT, amounted to PLN 34.6 billion, which represents an increase of almost 7%. in relation to the plan and by 16.4% in relation to 2017 In 2018, it was possible to achieve a higher than assumed fossil tax revenue by nearly 31%. In 2018, expenditure planned in the amount of PLN 397.2 billion, expenditure realized, was nearly 15 billion higher than expenditure in 2017. The budget deficit for 2018 amounted to 10.4 billion and was lower than the plan by 31 billion PLN. This record low budget deficit has contributed to reducing public debt to 48.9% and to ensure the sustainability of public finances. According to GUS estimates, labour productivity in Poland measured by production sold for one employee was 3,1% in 2018 higher than a year ago. Preliminary data from the European Commission show that labour productivity in Poland has increased by 6.2% in 2018 by an hour, compared to 2017, while the European Union average has only increased by 0.7%. So Poland is still catching up on the highly developed economies of Western Europe. This Decision shall enter Poland received PLN 66.3 billion from the European Union budget, while the European Union's budget paid PLN 18.7 billion. This means that the Polish economy gained net funds of PLN 47.6 billion. Budget expenditure for European Union funds amounted to PLN 66.8 billion in 2018. These were expenditures higher by PLN 18.8 billion compared to 2017, i.e. by more than 39%. In 2018, a limit of 200 billion guarantees was set in the Budget Act, which was only used in 5.8%. which should be assessed positively. Net lending needs amounted to only 1.3 billion and were lower than planned by PLN 62 billion. The overall deficit had a major impact on the development of loan needs. The deficit of the state budget, as I mentioned, was only 10.4 billion, while the deficit of the budget of the European Union, instead of the planned amount of PLN 15.5 billion, amounted to PLN 3.5 billion. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The Public Finance Commission considered the report on the implementation of the State budget in 2018 at meetings held from 27 June to 4 July inclusive. During the preparation of the report on the implementation of the State budget for 2018, the committee considered the budgetary parts of its remit, including those which were the subject of other committees, in accordance with the order of the Marshal of the Sejm. The other committees have submitted 25 opinions to the committee. All the opinions submitted were positive. During her meetings, the Committee on Public Finance took note of the opinions of other committees, the opinions of MEPs, kodeferents and the positions of the Supreme Audit Chamber. Members of the Committee on Public Finance have asked questions. There's been a long discussion. If the information and material submitted did not raise objections, the discussion did not always take place. During the meetings of the Committee on Public Finance, additional explanations were provided by representatives of various departments. Therefore, on 4 July the committee adopted the draft resolution proposed by the committee bureau following the vote. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Point I proposes that the High House adopt a report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2018 and, in point II, that discharge be given to the Council of Ministers for that period. On the other hand, in point III, the committee proposes to ask the authorising officers of the various budgetary parts to take action to address the irregularities identified by the Supreme Audit Chamber. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u5	Thank you, sir. I ask the President of the Supreme Audit Chamber of Krzysztof Kwiatkowski to present an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy assumptions in 2018 and an opinion on discharge for the Council of Ministers.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In fulfilling the obligation under Article. 204 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, I have the honour to present to the High House an analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy assumptions in 2018 together with the opinion of the College of the Supreme Audit Chamber on discharge for the Council of Ministers for 2018. The Supreme Audit Chamber, after carrying out the audit in 267 units, positively assessed the implementation of the State budget for 2018. The Supreme Audit Chamber also stated that the Council of Ministers' report on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2018 provides reliable information and data on the amount of revenue, expenditure, debts and liabilities and the result of the State budget in all material respects. [[President of the Supreme Chamber of Control Krzysztof Kwiatkowski illustrates his presentation displayed on the board]] The audit covered almost all the budget managers, the largest state-owned special-purpose funds, selected executive agencies and public legal entities and other bodies implementing public expenditure, including 96 grant beneficiaries. The detailed results of the audit were presented in separate information provided to the Marshal and to the Sejm committees in mid-June. Ladies and gentlemen, you have worked at the meetings of the Sejm committees, dealing with information on the implementation of budgets in the various ministries and central offices. Thus, in terms of post-control assessments in the information for 2018 positive assessments were clearly prevailing. Of the 141 post-control assessments, there were 116, or more than 80%. In addition, we have issued 22 descriptive assessments and 3 negative assessments. The descriptive assessments were formulated where irregularities were found, the scale and nature of which resulted in a reduction in the assessment. Compared to 2017, the number of positive assessments increased, there were fewer descriptive assessments and the number of negative assessments remained unchanged. In 2018, like last year, we negatively assessed the implementation of the plans of two state-owned special-purpose funds, the Fund - Central Registration of Vehicles and Drivers and the Fund for Hurtful Aid and Post-Penitentiary Assistance, so-called. The justice fund. The negative evaluation was also received by the Western Institute of Higher Education, which had not been audited a year before. Zygmunt Wojciechowski. The negative assessment in the case of the Fund - Central Vehicle and Driver Registration, CEPiK, was issued due to a non-intended order from the Central Information Technology Centre to perform the system stabilisation service and to conclude an unfavourable agreement to terminate the system construction contract. The justification for the negative assessment of the implementation of the financial plan of the Fund for Hurt Aid and Post-Penitentiary Assistance was the marginalisation of the basic objectives of the Fund and the allocation of a significant proportion of its revenue to activities not related to the victim. The NIK has also established that the Fund has regularly used a specific non-competition method for granting grants for tasks, which was contrary to the overriding principles of the Public Finance Act. At the Western Institute. Zygmunt Wojciechowski found significant irregularities involving incorrect determination and enforcement of charges for the rental of office premises and public spending, as well as violations of the provisions of the Public Procurement Law. We have prepared descriptive evaluations in 19 budgetary parts, as well as such assessments were presented in the case of the implementation of the financial plan of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management, the Polish Space Agency and the Agency for the Protection of Medical Technologies and Tarification. The opinion on the report of the Council of Ministers on the implementation of the State budget was formulated primarily on the basis of an examination of the accounts and budgetary statements. We checked the accounts with 20 directors, whose total expenditure amounted to almost 70%. expenditure of the State budget and of the budget of European funds. We gave a positive opinion on the correctness of the accounts in 16 directors, in 3 more we gave an opinion in descriptive form, and in the case of 1 editor of the ledger, the opinion was negative. We established in the area of accounts with this authorising officer, and this was the District Infrastructure Board in Zielona Góra, among others. irregularities related to the non-compliance of accounting policies with the amended provisions from January 2018 There were errors in general in those units in the eligibility and inclusion of claims on budgetary revenue or in the absence of certain mandatory elements from accounting records. In the case of a negative entity, none of the 202 audited accounting records contained a set of information required by the Accounting Act. We carried out an examination of the annual accounts with 276 directors, including 103 budget managers and 173 so-called directors. Third degree. We gave a positive opinion on the correctness of the reports in 93% of the contributors. The irregularities identified concerned in particular the recording of budgetary revenue claims, insufficient management control mechanisms that could not prevent irregularities. These irregularities were corrected during the audit or did not have a significant impact on the correctness of the data shown in the Council of Ministers' report on the implementation of the State budget. Ladies and gentlemen! The state budget, the budget of the European funds and the financial plans of the non-budget units of the public finance sector have been implemented in accordance with the budget law. The total revenue of the State budget and of the European budget amounted to in 2018. PLN 443 400 million and were higher than the revenue generated in the previous year by PLN 45 400 million. The revenue of the state budget amounted to 380 billion in 2018 and was almost 30 billion higher than that of the previous year and more than 24 billion higher than the projected revenue. Another year in a row the share of tax revenues in the revenue of the state budget has increased. In 2018, it was almost 92%. The high performance of tax revenues consisted mainly of higher than projected revenue from taxes on goods and services and income taxes. This was influenced by both the favourable economic situation of the country as well as the implemented sealing solutions of the tax system. However, tax arrears remained at a high level. At the end of 2018, the tax arrears amounted to 101 billion, although their growth rate was lower than in previous years. The rate of implementation of past years has decreased and amounted to 4% , while in 2018 it amounted to 4.6%. Due to the ineffective execution, enforcement authorities discontinued enforcement proceedings in 2018 in relation to the backlog of about PLN 13 billion. As in 2017, there was a low ratio of the amount of tax arrears enforced to the overall amount of tax arrears. Non-tax revenues in 2018 amounted to PLN 28 900 million and were lower than in 2016. This was mainly due to the lack of payment of the profit of the National Bank of Poland. Non-tax revenues from other sources were higher or similar to those realised in 2017. European revenue in 2018 amounted to PLN 63 300 million and accounted for almost 98% the plan set out in the Budget Act, and compared with the implementation in 2017, were almost PLN 16 billion higher. As in previous years, their structure was dominated by revenues realised under national and regional operational programmes, the financial perspective and the common agricultural policy for the period 2014-2020. Ladies and gentlemen! NIK maintains its position already expressed in previous years on the purpose of amending the functioning revenue system of the European budget, in order to ensure that, in accordance with the principle of spot accounting of income operations, the funds that entered Poland were already included in the revenue of the European budget at the date of their impact. High Seym! The expenditure of the state budget and the budget of European funds amounted to a total of PLN 457 300 million and was PLN 33.5 billion higher than the expenditure incurred a year before. In the amount of PLN 5100 million, i.e. almost four times the previous year, were expenditure which did not expire after the end of the financial year in 2018. In 2018, as in previous years, the largest funds were allocated to compulsory social security, general subsidy for local government units, family, national defence, transport and communication and agriculture. State budget expenditure amounted to a total of PLN 390 500 million, or 98.3% the limit laid down in the Budget Act. The nearly 4-percent increase in state budget expenditure recorded in 2018 included all generic groups, with the exception of expenditure on the handling of the Treasury debt, as these were reduced by 0.5%. NIK audited the legality, economy, purposefulness and reliability of state budget expenditure for a total amount of PLN 95 400 million. Most of the expenditure examined was properly incurred. The irregularities found were presented in the information on the results of the State budget implementation control. European budget expenditure amounted to PLN 66 900 million, i.e. 96.4% the plan after the changes, and they were almost 19 billion higher than the expenditure incurred in 2017. By the end of 2018, the amount of European Union funding resulting from Cohesion Policy co-financing agreements reached PLN 233.5 billion and was almost 36% higher than a year ago. This amount was almost 71% allocation for the years 2014 to 2020 The sum of eligible expenditure shown in the statements and declarations of expenditure and applications for payment in part co-financed by the European Union submitted to the European Commission by the end of 2018 under the national and regional operational programmes amounted to PLN 82 800 million and accounted for 25% allocation for the years 2014 to 2020 This indicator was twice as high as a year ago. We note the different stages of the implementation of regional operational programmes. The lowest cultivation rate to the value of the allocation was achieved in the war. Pomeranian and Opole, and the lowest in the war. Kujawsko-Pomorskie and Podlaskie. Ladies and gentlemen! The balance of financial accounts between Poland and the European Union in 2018 amounted to PLN 11 300 million, and since 2004 it amounted to PLN 107 500 million. Ladies and gentlemen! In 2018, the government budget deficit amounted to PLN 10 400 million and the European budget amounted to PLN 3.5 billion. The total deficit of the state budget and the budget of European funds amounted to PLN 13 900 million and was lower by 43 billion than planned and lower by PLN 11 800 million than in the previous year. The government budget deficit was decided primarily by spending nearly 11 billion in December 2018 in connection with the implementation of the revised around budget law at the end of the year. The Act adopted a financing arrangement in 2018 for the tasks to be carried out in 2019 which, according to NIK, contradict the principle of annuality of the budget. The net lending needs of the state budget amounted to PLN 1300 million and were 62 billion lower than planned. Their amount was mainly due to more favourable economic conditions than those assumed at the drafting stage of the budget law, as well as to the use of part of the funds raised in the framework of the consolidation of gold and currency. Ladies and gentlemen! As in previous years, the addition to the Social Insurance Fund of the loss of contributions provided by the Social Insurance Institution to open pension funds was PLN 3300 million. It was classified as transfers of funds from the State budget rather than expenditure. However, taking into account the repeatedly notified recommendation of the NIK, the Law of 9 November 2018 on specific arrangements for the implementation of the Budget Law for 2019 adopted a solution whereby in 2019 funds for this purpose will be transferred to the Social Insurance Fund in the form of grants. In the NIK's assessment, this will increase the transparency of public finances. It is also appropriate to recognise this solution as a permanent principle. With effect from 1 January 2018, loans granted to the Social Insurance Fund were decommitted from the State budget for the period 2014 to 2016, of which [[♪ The bell ♪]] the repayment deadline was 31 March 2019 in the total amount of PLN 7200 million. The sum of loans decommitted in 2017 and 2018 amounted to PLN 46 300 million. Ladies and gentlemen! Public debt, sovereign debt, government debt and government debt to gross domestic product developed below planned levels in 2018 and decreased in relation to the values of those indicators recorded in 2017. In 2018, the Treasury's debt increased by almost PLN 26 billion, which was affected in particular by the decrease in the value of the gold vis-à-vis the main currencies and the decisions of the Minister of Finance to accumulate higher funds in bank accounts at the end of 2018 than at the end of the previous year. The financial result of the public finance sector was better than in previous years. The report of the Council of Ministers on the implementation of the State budget for the period from 1 January to 31 December 2018 shows that in 2018 the revenue of the public finance sector amounted to PLN 864 900 million and expenditure - PLN 861 200 million. For the first time since 2007, a surplus of PLN 3700 million has occurred in the public finance sector. In 2018, as in previous years, according to NIK, the revenue and performance of the public finance sector was wrongly calculated. Revenue and result were overestimated by PLN 3300 million. The correctly calculated surplus of the public finance sector was PLN 400 million. The reason for demonstrating incorrect data was to include in the revenue of the public sector funds transferred from the State budget to the Social Insurance Fund for the compensation of contributions lost to open pension funds. General government result calculated according to Union methodology it was negative and amounted to PLN 8 billion, and in relation to GDP it was minus 0.4%. This was better than the average result in the European Union minus 0.6%. Ladies and gentlemen! And finally, the major irregularities. The irregularities identified by the NIK in the framework of the State budget implementation audit in 2018 have not changed significantly compared to previous years and, due to their scale, have not had a significant impact on the correctness of the collection and spending of public funds in general. These were mainly due to errors in accounting and reporting, ineffective management control, errors in public procurement, and incorrect transfer and use of grant funds. The NIK also directed the budget proposals in the Task Force, noting that it is not an effective tool for planning and implementing public expenditure. We have also pointed to the need to strengthen the supervision of the budgetary responsibilities of public spending. High Seym! After carrying out the audit at the National Bank of Poland, NIK positively assessed the implementation of monetary policy objectives. Average annual inflation in 2018 remained in the range of deviations from the inflation target and amounted to 1.6% , whereas in the previous year - 2%. Ladies and gentlemen! The NIK College, having read the entire analysis of the implementation of the state budget and monetary policy objectives in 2018, adopted a resolution in which it expressed a positive opinion on discharge to the Council of Ministers. The NIK College, taking into account the results of the review of the implementation of the State budget, pointed out the need to enforce tax arrears more effectively and prevent them from rising, to improve the methods of planning expenditure, including the appropriations included in the special provisions, and to continue to review expenditure and use its results when drawing up budgetary laws. Thank you for your attention. Of course, I will be available with my colleagues in the context of specific questions that may arise after committee meetings, which dealt in detail with reports in individual ministries and central offices. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u9	Thank you, Minister. I would like to ask Mrs Gabriela Masłowska to present a report on the information provided by the President of the Council of Ministers on the guarantees and guarantees given in 2018 by the State Treasury, certain legal persons and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u10	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Gentlemen of Ministers! The information on guarantees and guarantees provided by the State Treasury, some legal persons and Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego presented by the Council of Ministers is part of the control of the state of public finances held by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. It has been drawn up since 1998. It is intended to implement the provisions of the Law of 8 May 1997 on Guarantees and Guarantees provided by the State Treasury and certain legal persons, specifically the provisions of the Article. 46. The Council of Ministers' information on guarantees and guarantees given in 2018 was presented and discussed at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance on 27 June this year. According to the above. information, in 2018 six State Treasury guarantees were granted for a total amount of approx. PLN 11.6 billion. These guarantees were granted for liabilities incurred by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego to the burden of the National Road Fund in order to provide funds for the implementation of the Fund's tasks and for commitments incurred by PKP Polskie Linie Kolejowe SA in order to co-finance the investment of railway infrastructure. The potential non-necessary liabilities of the State Treasury for the guarantees and guarantees granted amounted to about PLN 108,8 billion as at 31 December 2018 and decreased by 6.9% compared to 2017. Unrequired commitments are designed to secure, are set in the form of a guarantee limit and guarantees in the Budget Act. As regards 2018, it was set at PLN 200 billion. The main factor that affected the establishment of a limit on guarantees and guarantees for 2018 in the Budget Act was the possibility of using such a limit in the event of a possible deterioration of the conditions of operation of the Polish financial system, e.g. in the face of a possible financial and economic crisis, by supporting entities in the sector and, as regards certain institutions, using these guarantees to stabilise the institutions in the event of such a need. They are therefore preventive in nature. As there was no need to use the expected limit for this purpose in 2018, this situation should be assessed positively. I stress: in 2018 the Treasury did not spend funds to repay guaranteed and guaranteed liabilities. In 2018 Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego in the framework of the Entrepreneurship Support Programme gave around 40,000. guarantees and economic guarantees in portfolio mode for a total amount of approx. PLN 11.7 billion, including approx. 35 thousand. guarantees so called Guarantee lines PLD for the total amount of approx. 10,2 billion PLN. In the light of the information provided by the Council of Ministers of the Committee on Public Finance, it should be considered that the guarantees and guarantees provided by the Treasury and the Bank of National Farms under government programmes do not pose a risk to public finances, but at the same time constitute an important instrument for supporting economic processes, mainly in the field of road and rail infrastructure, as well as the activities of small and medium-sized enterprises. After hearing the information and after the discussion, the Committee on Public Finance issued a favourable opinion on this information. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u11	Thank you, Member. I ask the President of the National Bank of Poland, Mr Adam Glapiński, to present a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2018.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u12	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As President of the National Bank of Poland and Chairman of the Monetary Policy Council, I have the honour to submit to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland a report on the implementation of monetary policy assumptions for 2018 and a report on the activities of the National Bank of Poland in 2018. During today's speech, I would like to focus on the most important actions taken by the National Bank of Poland in 2018. Mr. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! According to the Constitution, the National Bank of Poland is responsible for the value of Polish money. In addition, the NBP Act indicates that the primary objective of NBP's activities is to maintain a stable level of prices while supporting the government's economic policy, unless this limits the NBP's basic objective. The macroeconomic results of the Polish economy clearly indicate that the National Bank of Poland is exemplary in fulfilling its constitutional and statutory obligations. Inflation is in line with the NBP's inflation target and Poles have confidence in the gold. At the same time, growth is high and sustainable and the banking sector is functioning efficiently and stable. I would like to remind you that NBP is committed to maintaining price stability, using the so-called strategy. the inflation target. Since 2004, the NBP's inflation target has consistently been 2,5%. , plus 1 percentage point and inflation in Poland during this period was On average, only 2.0%. At the same time, economic growth has been rapid but sustainable in recent years. This points to the very high effectiveness of the inflation target strategy in ensuring long-term price stability while promoting sustainable economic growth. The Monetary Policy Council in 2018 maintained NBP interest rates unchanged, including a reference rate of 1,5%. Such an action allowed another year in a row to effectively implement the constitutional and statutory obligations of the NBP. This is demonstrated above all by the low inflation rate over the long term, which is the central bank's main indicator of effectiveness. In the last 3 years, i.e. during the period when I am privileged to be president of NBP, inflation has been on average 1.4%. It is now slightly higher, to which the price increase of some food products on the world markets and, as a result, also on the domestic market significantly contributes. However, inflation continues to be in line with the NBP's inflation target and inflation has been on average 1.8% since the beginning of this year. Importantly, monetary policy supports the maintenance of high but at the same time sustainable economic growth. Last year, GDP growth was 5.1% , and in the first quarter of this year. 4.7%. The Polish economy is therefore one of the fastest growing economies in the European Union. At the same time, there is no increase in macroeconomic imbalances in our country. Economic growth in Poland is based on both consumption and investment, with steadily growing exports. Growth in consumption supported by rising employment and low unemployment, as well as rising wages raises the standard of living of Poles and allows them to benefit from the fruits of economic development. At the same time, a significant increase in investment lays the foundations for stable production growth in the future. Finally, despite trade disputes and the decline in world trade, Polish exports continue to grow dynamically. Particularly important, monetary policy to maintain high and sustainable economic growth supports the opportunities to increase tax revenues from the public finance sector needed to finance public expenditure in a stable manner. In addition, the stabilisation of NBP's interest rates contributes to a decrease in the cost of handling foreign debt, to a decrease in the cost of handling public debt. In this way, NBP carries out its second statutory task, i.e. supports the economic policy of the government. Thanks to the Responsible and Conservative Monetary Policy of the NBP, we have ensured not only price stability but also a broadly understood macroeconomic balance. It is very important because it reduces the risk of crisis and rising unemployment and strengthens the resilience of the Polish economy to any potential external shocks. In this context, it is worth mentioning that lending has been increasing for several years at an appropriate pace, which on the one hand supports economic growth and, on the other hand, does not jeopardise the stability of the financial system. As a consequence, the non-financial debt of the credit sector in relation to GDP remains stable and our banking system is functioning effectively. Moreover, despite a very good domestic economy, the surplus in foreign trade remains and the total balance in the current and capital account is positive. As a result, foreign debt in relation to GDP is steadily decreasing. In such circumstances and in a situation of foreseeable monetary policy, the rate of gold despite the periodic strong fluctuations of certain currencies, especially from so-called. emerging markets, has been stable for a long time. Currently, the volatility of the golden course has remained at the lowest level for decades. This is a sign of high confidence in the gold not only from Poles, but also from foreign investors. The high credibility of the gold is one of the proofs of the effectiveness of the monetary policy of the National Bank of Poland, which is particularly welcome in this year, in which we celebrate the centenary of establishing the Polish gold as a national currency. The NBP's monetary policy was implemented in 2018 on the basis of a set of instruments established by the Monetary Policy Council for 2018. The conditions prevailing on domestic and foreign financial markets did not require NBP to apply other than the standard monetary policy instruments. The liquidity of the banking sector was absorbed by the central bank through open market operations. Monetary policy was conducted effectively and effectively, as evidenced by a slight deviation of the Polonia rate from the NBP reference rate. Mr. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! A stable financial system is a prerequisite for sustainable economic development and long-term price stability. The statutory task of NBP for the stability of the financial system includes eliminating and reducing systemic risk and shaping the conditions necessary for the development of the banking system. Financial stability in our country largely depends on the security of the banking sector, whose assets account for more than 70% assets of national financial institutions and which is the main national source of financing for the economy in Poland. As in previous years, debt levels and credit growth rates were moderate as well in 2018. They were not a source of imbalances in Poland's economy and financial system, nor were they a barrier to economic growth. The growth of loans to the non-financial sector in 2018 after taking into account the exchange rate changes was stable at 5.8% , only slightly lower than nominal GDP growth. Among the most important barriers to the development of the companies indicated in the cyclical NBP survey, there were no restrictions on access to bank financing. On the other hand, the dynamics of loans to households should be seen particularly in the context of the two-digit growth rate of housing loans in PLN and almost 90% of the dynamics of consumer loans. The banks used stable sources, mainly household deposits, to finance their lending. The trend in the quality of the credit portfolio did not differ significantly from those observed a year before. The Polish banking system was characterized by good capital equipment, adequate to the scale and nature of the business, as well as a relatively low level of leverage. During the period considered, the banks continued to increase their own funds, mainly from retained earnings. In the banking sector, the total capital ratio reached 18.3% at the end of 2018, compared to 18.0% at the end of 2017, with a minimum level of 8%. Most banks met the regulatory requirements, including the maintenance of additional capital buffers. The results of stress tests also indicate that, due to consistent accumulation of capital, banks in Poland remain relatively resistant to negative shocks. Even in the event of a very negative macroeconomic situation, only a small group of banks would suffer capital shortages compared to minimum regulatory capital requirements. However, the NBP identifies the following developments in the banking system or its surroundings, which require special attention. Firstly, the granting of new housing loans under conditions that have persisted for several years at low credit prices and high real estate market activity in recent quarters. This area requires observation in terms of risk of excessive expansion. At the same time, we estimate that the risk is limited due to the supervisory and relatively conservative policies taken in recent years by the banks themselves. Secondly, the low profitability of some banks and its long-term consequences. The profitability of Polish banks remains above the average in the banking sector throughout the European Union, but in recent years it has fallen significantly and remains below the estimated cost of raising capital on the market. In addition, it is worth noting that the technological changes observed require banks to continue their modernisation processes, for which more and more financial expenditure is needed. Thirdly, the development challenges of the cooperative banking sector. The cooperative banks sector as a whole is stable, albeit facing short- and long-term challenges. Cooperative banks mostly comply with supervisory capital and liquidity requirements. In some cooperative banks, however, the poor quality and high concentration of the credit portfolio are concerned with the relatively low level of credit coverage at risk of write-downs. The low efficiency of the business model and the low level of integration of the sector are, in turn, a challenge to their development in the medium term. Therefore, the NBP has long pointed out the need to increase the integration of cooperative banks, including institutional protection systems, and the need to strengthen association banks, inter alia. by improving the quality of risk management and increasing their capital equipment. In conclusion, according to NBP's assessment, both the Polish financial system and its economic environment remained balanced in 2018. This assessment was also shared by the International Monetary Fund and the World Bank mission in a report on Poland published at the beginning of this year. Mr. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! In 2918. The NBP participated in the work of the Financial Stability Committee in two formulas: macro-prudential supervision and crisis management. In addition to the NBP, the Committee is represented by the Ministry of Finance, the Financial Supervision Commission and the Bank Guarantee Fund. As President of the NBP, I chair the Financial Stability Committee in the macro-prudential formula, whose main task is to identify and reduce systemic risk. In 2018, six meetings of the Financial Stability Committee were held in this formula. A detailed description of the Committee's activities in 2018 was presented and provided to the State, namely: information on the activities of the Financial Stability Committee as a macro-prudential body. At the beginning of July, I handed this report over to the Marshal of the Sejm. The NBP also cooperated within the Financial Stability Committee in a crisis management formula chaired by the Minister of Finance. In 2018, 12 meetings of the Committee were held in this formula, during which the economic and financial situation of specific banks and measures to maintain stability in the financial system were discussed. As you know, in 2018 The NBP provided commercial banks with significant support in the form of refinancing loans of a total amount of PLN 5840 million, which allowed them to stabilise their liquidity situation while at the same time protecting the stability of the entire sector. [[♪ The bell ♪]] These loans were fully repaid in the first quarter of 2019. [[Applause]] In addition, in order to support the implementation of resolution programmes or recovery plan, the NBP exempted four banks from minimum reserve requirements in 2018. At the end of 2018, the official reserve assets of the National Bank of Poland amounted to EUR 102.3 billion. The priority in managing deficit reserves is, of course, to ensure a high level of security for the funds invested and their proper liquidity. Only after these conditions have been met will measures be taken to increase the profitability of reserves. However, in 2018, the rate of return on reserves calculated in PLN was 5.9%. This was due to the strengthening of most reserve currencies vis-à-vis the gold. On the other hand, the rate of return in the currency of the instruments alone was 1,1%. The NBP's strategic decisions in the area of deficit reserve management include an increase in the stock of gold by the NBP. In 2018 we purchased 25,7 t gold. As you know, in 2019 we bought another 100 tons of gold. [[Applause]] Currently, the NBP's gold stock is 228.6 t. These resources have almost doubled over Sweden's resources, compared with the Belgian ones and have come much closer to Austrian and Spanish. As a result of purchases made in 2018 and 2019, NBP moved in the ranking of central banks according to the size of gold resources from 34th to 22nd position in the world and from 15th place in Europe, ahead of all countries in the region. At the same time, the share of gold in Poland's foreign exchange reserves increased from around 4% at the end of 2017 to almost 10% , bringing the NBP closer in terms of the share of gold in foreign exchange reserves to the average in central banks around the world, but on average in European banks That's still 20%, so twice as much. The increase in Polish gold resources was, of course, possible thanks to the exceptionally good economic situation of the country and the strong increase in foreign exchange reserves. Over the last 5 years, Poland's foreign exchange reserves have increased by more than EUR 30 billion. This strengthens the financial security of Poland, strengthens the credibility and financial strength of our country, even in extremely unfavourable conditions. The purchased gold has been deposited in the Bank of England in the form of a London Good Delivery staff. The ability to store gold in the Bank of England, as you know, benefits most of the contractors active in the world gold market. This allows not only to store its resources safely, but also to increase their profitability. In 2018, the revenue from investments in gold amounted to 2.7 million euro, i.e. approximately 11.4 million PLN. However, I would like to stress that because of the strategic nature of gold and the market conditions, gold resources are not seen as an important source of income for central banks. In view of this, the Management Board of the NBP decided to diversify its storage sites and transfer almost half of the current gold stock, i.e. Approximately 100 t, to the NBP vaults. [[Applause]] This operation shall be carried out in accordance with a timetable to ensure its safety. We will provide you with information on its completion as soon as it is completed. The gold that will remain in London, i.e. Approximately 129 t, will be invested in the current way. The NBP's financial statements for 2018 received a positive opinion from the statutory auditor. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u164	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to remind you that the Act on Special Economic Zones, which was introduced in the original version in 1994, was used to stimulate investment, increase investment size, encourage investors to invest in Poland, increase jobs. It generated 14 special economic zones, brought many good, positive, positive economic impacts. A new law entered into force last year. After the evaluation of the functioning of the zones, we prepared a new law that extended the scope of the zone privileges, making Poland the whole special economic zone. I therefore report to you on the functioning of this law in the year in which the new legislation entered into force. We can say that 2018 was characterised by a higher dynamic of parameters showing the effects of special economic zones in almost all areas, both in terms of investment expenditure, employment and new jobs created. In the case of investment investments, and these are probably the most interesting ones, and the number of new jobs in relation to 2017 exceeded 10%. At the same time, I would like to remind you that our amendment to this law has also taken into account the changing conditions. In the case of automation of production, industrial automation, the number of jobs can no longer be the basic parameter which is considered to grant zone permit, zone privilege, long-term tax credit, but precisely the quality of the investment, the size of this investment, the location of this investment and the link between investment and local R &amp; D infrastructure. At the end of 2018, the total area of 14 zones amounted to almost 23 000. ha and was larger than the previous year by 223 ha. At the end of 2018, the zones included areas located in 186 cities and 308 municipalities. The average level of zoning was 63%, while the diversity of land use, which has been observed for many years, is quite significant in the different zones. At the end of 2018 entrepreneurs had almost 2600 valid business permits in zones. The automotive industry, which accounts for almost 25% of the investment, has been the largest share for many years. Further locations include rubber and plastics manufacturers and entrepreneurs in the industry, and the next ones include metal products manufacturers. The most funds, the largest part of this envelope was invested by the municipalities, then management companies, and the other important investor remained the General Directorate of National Roads and Motorways. Last year is another good year for the functioning of special economic zones, the number of investors. I will also remind you that recently published rankings, among others. Last year also one of the Polish special zones, the Łódź Special Economic Zone, was considered the best in terms of investment for innovators. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u168	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present an opinion on the implementation of the Act on Special Economic Zones as at 31 December 2018, contained in print No 3491. The above document contains information on the functioning of special economic zones with the following issues: the location and level of development of each zone, the effects of the zones taking into account the number of permits granted, the jobs created, the value and the industry structure of the investments made and the countries of origin of capital, as well as data on the activities of companies managing zones. Currently there are 14 special economic zones in Poland. Each zone is an administratively separate part of the territory of Poland, where entrepreneurs making new investments benefit from public aid in the form of tax exemptions in accordance with the principles fully compatible with European Union law in force on the date of the authorisation. The most important information on the activities of special economic zones in this document is the following. In 2018, the total area of zones increased by 222.9 ha, i.e. up to level 22 883.6 ha, and entrepreneurs had 2535 valid business permits in the zones. The accumulated value of the invested capital amounted to nearly PLN 119.2 billion, which means an increase of more than PLN 12.6 billion, or almost 12%. As of the end of 2017, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and At the end of 2018, investors employed more than 379 thousand. employees. Compared to 2017, their number increased by 26 thousand, i.e. 7.4%. Invested in more than 60% in zones the percentage came from five countries, i.e. from Poland, Germany, the Netherlands, Luxembourg and the USA. The largest share in the cumulative value of the investment was by automotive companies - more than 24%, followed by rubber and plastics producers - almost 9,7% metal products - approx. 7,7% , and the woodworking industry, which also bounces out here. 302.4 billion PLN was spent on the construction of infrastructure in the zone, of which 28.1% of the expenditure of management companies in the special economic zone was spent on the construction of infrastructure in the zone. , and other expenditures were carried out by municipalities, media gestors and the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways. In conclusion, these figures and given that the dynamics of total employment investment and new jobs were positive and the value of investments and the number of new jobs increased by 10%, it can be concluded that the balance of the activities of the management of special economic zones is positive. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice recommends that the High Sejm adopt information on the implementation of the Act on Special Economic Zones. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u207	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! This project assumes that the company's agreement will now be allowed to participate in the shareholders' meeting using teleconferences or teleconferences. From now on, it will be permitted to broadcast the meetings of the shareholders in real time and to communicate bilaterally in real time, in which the partners may express themselves in the course of the meetings of the shareholders, while staying in a place other than the meeting of the shareholders. In the justification of the project we read that the teleconferencing consists of transmitting by electronic means the meetings held at the place specified in the agreement of the company to other places where the partners are gathered. On the other hand, the videoconference is a kind of electronic connection between partners staying in different locations. A partner may be in a place other than the place of the meeting, but at a place indicated by the company. At a videoconference, the partner may join it from any place having access to electronic communications used to conduct a videoconference. According to the project, however, the adoption of an electronic form of assembly is not to exempt the company from determining the place where the managing partner meeting, together with the minutes and possible bodies of the company, will physically reside. High Izbo! The proposed solution will improve the functioning of a wide range of limited liability companies, with the current easily accessible technological opportunities. I think this is particularly important in the face of widespread computerisation and the ability to perform tasks remotely. The undoubted advantage of the proposed solutions is technological neutrality, i.e. leaving economic operators free to choose technological measures. In Poland we have more than 450,000. companies that will be able to benefit from this solution. The current version of the draft amendment uses a phrase referring to bilateral communication. Interested parties, i.e. entrepreneurs, point out that it would be more appropriate to use the phrase: multilateral communication, which would avoid any doubt if more than two partners participated in the video conference. In our opinion, this call deserves attention and consideration in the course of the work on the project, which we would like to encourage. On the other hand, in general, the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club - Coalition Poland will certainly not prevent this project from being adopted by the High House. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u227	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission on Changes in Codifications, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the government draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and certain other laws, print No 3541 and 3592. The Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications examined the draft law at its meeting on 4 July, adopted a draft with an amendment to the article. 328, which specifies the procedure and grounds for entering an entry in the register of shareholders, added § 5a as follows: When entering the register of shareholders, the entity keeping the register shall take into account the restrictions on the Regulation by action. The Commission has also adopted legislative, editorial and clarification amendments. I would like to remind you that the project aims to introduce a basis for compulsory dematerialisation of shares in public limited liability companies and limited liability companies. The proposed regulations will affect the establishment of a single legal framework by introducing an autonomous trading regime for shares in non-public companies, i.e. outside the organised market. The additional benefits of the dematerialisation process are mainly linked to the reduction of the costs of printing, storing and transporting securities documents - shares. It is also worth noting that dematerialisation minimises the risks associated with the loss of securities. At the same time, there is no reason for this not to apply to registered shares. The non-dematerialisation of registered shares would also result in a non-public company having to maintain two separate systems for registering shares: the register of shareholders referred to in the project and the stock book, which would not only be cumbersome and more costly for the company itself, but would also complicate the legal environment. The compulsory dematerialisation of shares causes that each action, whether a bearer action or a name action, has de facto the status of a registered share allowing the identification of a shareholder entitled to bearer shares rights in cases defined by law. It should also be noted that the project enhances transparency and the efficiency of the exchange of information in tax matters in accordance with the standards of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development. As a result of the work of the committees and experts, the rules have been clarified and they fulfil their role in the evaluation of the committees. High Izbo! The Commission proposes that the High Sejm adopt the attached draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The adopted system makes it possible to create such securities in an unlimited way, and if the problem is bearer securities, this problem had to be dealt with in a comprehensive way, and not just eliminated bearer shares from the Polish system. Therefore, the dematerialisation provided for in this Act will not solve, will not remove all the problems that caused the proposed act. The risks of abuse will still exist. Secondly, it may also be that the bearer shares will be owned by entity C, let's give it to a foreign entity, whether it's an investment fund or a foreign commercial law company, in which the bearer shares are governed by the law, is anonymous, and it will turn out that by the way in which you want to go towards this anonymity, I apologize very much, we will all bounce from the wall. We will not be able to check and determine who owns the units - this is called equity units in investment funds or settlement funds, or partners, whether they have shares or shares of companies that are e.g. foreign companies. So the intentions were probably noble and good, of course, the idea was to increase transparency - and for the sake of clarity: I am in favour of increasing transparency - but I do not know if the proposed solution is the best possible, but already at the second reading stage it can be pointed out that it will simply be ineffective, possible to circumvent. So the question is: What is the point of creating legislation that - already at the stage of creation - will be possible to circumvent? Unless the Minister or the Ministry may not be at the stage yet, but perhaps in the Senate, or perhaps in the Senate - they will propose amendments to introduce or tighten up criminal regulations related to such circumventions that I have mentioned. So that I encourage you to consider whether these changes are really going to implement your intentions before you propose some solutions to us in Parliament, which are theoretically good, directionally also good ones, because in my opinion the intentions will not be realized. This will cause a bit of confusion, a new register will be created, which someone will probably have to handle, which will result in new costs, in fact, for the trading of securities little will change, it will become a little more difficult, it will become a little complicated. Possibly, because such effects may also be, some entrepreneurs who today, for example. they benefit from this form of bearer shares, they simply decide to transfer their capital elsewhere, where it will be possible to act in a form that will become impossible as of the date of entry into force of the Act. In parallel, another law amending the Commercial Companies Code is being examined. It must be pointed out that the art was not constructed properly by leaning on the bill under investigation. 328 § 2 of the Commercial Companies Code specifying a range of subjects entitled to keep a register of shareholders. At the same time, designers refer to the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, i.e. to entities that may hold securities accounts. However, no specific criteria shall be indicated for the selection of a third party to keep a stock register. The lack of restrictions in this respect is important in that it is de facto a third party to decide who becomes a shareholder of the company. In order to protect the interests of the company, it is appropriate to lay down specific criteria to be met by the above mentioned criteria. entities. In addition, the role of the registry operator in registering is not entirely clear. On the one hand, the draft indicates that the entity keeping the shareholder register is not required to examine the legality and validity of the documents justifying the entry of the alert, including the signature of the seller of shares or persons establishing a limited right in rem. On the other hand, it can do so on the basis of reasonable doubt. The mere wording of reasonable doubt is so indeterminate that it may be difficult to give it specific normative content. The provisions on this matter should be drafted in such a way that the obligations of the operator of the shareholder register are clearly defined. It is also worth pointing out the doubts raised by the issue of the effect of transferring rights from registered shares and linking this effect with an entry in the register. It seems doubtful to refer to as a de facto register kept by private entities subject only to a specific regime of financial supervision, but not to the status of public entities, without judicial review at the time of entry. Further comments give rise to the proposed article. 328 Commercial Companies Code, which provides for the disclosure of the shareholder register for the company and each of its shareholders. At the time of creation of the register, third parties, e.g. banks, brokerage houses, as well as state authorities therefore have access to a very wide range of information available to the company only so far. The proposed solution will also enable any interested person - for example: who will acquire one share of the company - to obtain information about the other shareholders. This may raise doubts regarding the interests and business secrets of the company as well as the confidentiality of shareholders themselves. The entry into force of the proposed amendments is linked to an increase in the operating costs of companies - e.g. driving [[♪ The bell ♪]] shareholder register, the obligation to have a website - which, especially in the case of public limited liability companies, will reduce their competitiveness. Mandatory dematerialisation of shares may discourage some investors from operating in the form of a public limited liability company or limited liability company. Due to comments submitted by the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party - Coalition Poland is against this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u249	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The problem we are dealing with at this point is very important for many reasons. The first, in addition to the certainty of trading, is the efficiency of commercial law companies, in this case public limited liability companies and limited liability companies, and the reduction of the costs of their activities in the near future, because the dematerialisation of the shares serves to ensure that the trading system for shares of non-public companies is reliable, efficient and, on the other hand, to guarantee less costs for the operation of the system. Today, each company must collect shares in the stock book, it must keep a stock book in paper form, at each issue of a new series it must perform a number of technical activities, not just printing, exchanging documentation. But these elements can go to a lameness because there are adequate systems that guarantee the certainty of entering the relevant information about the company, so that all shareholders can be sure of the company's rights, and the company can function smoothly and, if an investor is acquired, it can quickly carry out the investment process. There was a question of anti-money laundering. The recommendation of the OECD and the trends we see in Western European regulations aim to move away from the form of a paper survey of the status of companies in the field of shareholders and to move on to electronic systems. This is the case in the UK, Luxembourg, Switzerland, Czech Republic, Belgium. In some countries, the possibility of trading bearer shares was directly excluded. However, in our system we remain with this institution, of course by introducing an obligation to register these shares, so that all shares that can be traded and which generate rights in the company can be held in the register. It's a matter of record-keeping. We have 48 entities in Poland that can perform this service for companies. Competition between them will mean that this will not be the amount that will increase the operating costs of the public limited liability company due to the fact that the company already bears the high costs of organising the public limited liability book in its traditional form. It seems to me that discussing how to move on to modern forms of governance, modern forms of taking advantage of certain benefits of technology development, should rather be obvious; discussing the need to move on to dematerialisation wherever possible. In this case, it is possible and even desirable. The certainty as to who is the shareholder of the company is important not only for external entities but above all for shareholders themselves. The second issue of transparency. Publicity refers to the company and shareholders. All other entities which are involved in the registration process shall be subject to the applicable secrecy rules provided for by law. So there's no risk that information will circulate against the will, contrary to the law between different entities. Moreover, this is the trend in European Union and other legislation similar to that of the European Union in order to regulate these issues in the way we propose. Another problem was also raised. It seems that the Polish People's Party - Coalition Polish Parliamentary Club has submitted a reservation that the obligation of the public limited company to maintain a website on which the relevant information will be provided will result in the entrepreneurs not wanting to use the form of organization which is a public limited company. It seems to me that the discussion about whether a website is useful to entrepreneurs or not took place at least a dozen years ago, but referring to this objection, I am of the opinion that I do not share this view as much as possible. I believe that the majority of public limited liability companies carrying out a strong business activity benefit from this form of promotion of their services, which is the website, and the introduction of this obligation will not be severe for companies, but will only strengthen the possibility of offering services and products to customers. There is also a question of the idea of securities and their dematerialisation, bearer securities. The draft law makes it clear that any securities that relate to the rights in the field of public limited liability companies will be included in the register, so that they are also covered by this regulation. And the fact that we are discussing shares of a public limited liability company in this case, and not of other securities, comes directly from the scope of the submission. We're talking about the organization of the public limited liability company, the stock register directly related to the public limited liability company. It is not like the honourable Member said that this is a complete elimination of anonymity. However, when returning to this trade code from the interwar period, it is worth pointing out that one of the reasons for the operation of bearer securities is not the anonymity of the owner, it is the efficiency of trading. Yeah, she was like that, too. the reason for the operation of such documents so that the trading of these documents is efficient so that the seller and the buyer can make the transaction they desire as soon as possible, if that is their will. Today, these difficulties, including technological difficulties, which accompanied the turnover in the interwar twentieth anniversary, have disappeared in my opinion and if the creators of the Commercial Code - highly appreciated by many experts, law historians and practitioners to this day - had the opportunity to dematerialise the actions, I am convinced that they would benefit from it, because it is just how the system works better. And it is not true that there will be full anonymity of all bearer shares. The draft law does not remove a number of legal institutions allowing for various types of operations which will not be shown in that register. The trust institution will continue to function as far as possible. So to say that somebody is going to eliminate full anonymity in companies, if anybody wants it, is not true. On the other hand, Mr Grabowski pointed out how to get around it. There's nothing to care about. The purpose of the bill is not to liquidate bearer shares. The purpose of the Act is to create a system that will guarantee that everyone involved in the company and the interested person will invest in the company, a system of efficient, reliable acquisition of information about the status of shareholders in the company, using the technologies currently available. Besides, it is worth referring to the participants themselves. A CSD, a highly respected institution with no doubts as to how effectively it protects the information it is obliged to protect, has no loud scandals about it, supported the design proposed by the Ministry of Justice. Also positive opinions on the project were expressed by: KNF, Stock Exchange, business organisations, e.g. Leviatan, BCC, Chamber of Brokerage Houses, Center for Social and Economic Analysis - CASE, Center im. Adam Smith, the Union of Polish Banks. These are the institutions that have welcomed the direction of the changes proposed by the Ministry of Justice. I understand that sometimes there's a need to look for a hole in the whole thing, to criticize by force. Of course, it is always worth arguing about certain views in substance. However, in principle I am surprised by some speeches. It is believed that modern technologies, modern solutions that will improve the functioning of Polish enterprises, are generally wrong only because our government proposes them. I encourage a constructive conversation, especially that in a few years' time, I am convinced that when the House is leaning on other changes to the business company issue, no one will question that regulating the registration of shareholders through appropriate electronic registers is better for economic turnover. I'm gonna go back to the detailed questions that have come up. Question about the deadline, if for example. Someone will not report to the register until 2021. There is still a 5-year deadline within which it is possible to register bearer shares. If the bearer holder wishes to trade between 2021 and 2026, the bearer shares should first register and then, with the relevant certificate, they may dispose of their rights. As for the dialogue that was the subject of one of the questions, I think I have already spoken. The law was very well consulted, and work on it lasted almost 3 years. A number of the actors I have pointed out have responded positively. I think there are strong, positive opinions of a number of experts behind this project. In conclusion, I would like to say that the project submitted to the House for the dematerialisation of shares in public limited liability companies is a positive project, an important project which will strengthen the quality and transparency of trading in public limited liability companies. We use the available technologies so that entrepreneurs who use this form of activity can easily obtain investments and trading efficiently and at the same time reduce the costs resulting from the traditional way of keeping a stock book. For this reason, I ask the High House to support this bill. At the same time, we declare our willingness to talk further in this regard.
