ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u291	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I was pleased to hear your speeches and your speeches. Such debates are always watched by people with disabilities - on the Internet or on television. They certainly listened with interest to Members who showed their empathy and sensitivity. They will certainly visit their parliamentary offices many times, hoping that these Members will take care of their situation. As far as specific, substantive questions are concerned, you will allow me to address them first and foremost. Mr Piechota asked about the employment of people with disabilities. The employment rate in 2016 - I only go back to 2016 - was 26.8%. In 2018, it was 28.3%. As far as unemployment is concerned, in 2016, it was 11.6, today it is 7.2. In fact, the professional activity of people with disabilities is increasing. On the other hand, I agree with what the Member said - society is getting older. We have more and more pensioners, including those with disabilities. The number of persons of working age is smaller and can be spoken of in absolute terms. I'll take another note. Excuse me. I saw Mr Piechota and remembered that I had explanations about what was so worrying him yesterday, that is that the Committee on Public Finance will not be dealing with changes in the budget. I'll answer that. The Public Finance Act provides that the Fund's financial plan may be amended, and for the special purpose fund, of course the financial management of the special purpose fund is based on the annual financial plan. The costs of the State-owned special-purpose fund may be covered only by the funds at their disposal covering current revenues. The financial plan of the Funds shall be drawn up by the authorising officer and included in the draft budget law submitted in the Sejm. The financial plan of the fund included in the budget act adopted shall form the basis for the financial management of the fund manager during the year. In the case of amendments introduced by the law relating to the addition of a new task of the Fund and indicating the sources of its financing, the amendments to the plan are indicated directly in the text of the Act, which is assessed by the Sejm committees and adopted by the Sejm, by the entire High Chamber. Thus, the revision of the fund's financial plan results directly from the power of the law and not from the decision of the fund's authorising officer. Consequently, in such cases the provision on public finances for the opinion of the Sejm Committee, which would in this case be consulted on the decision of the Sejm, and not on the decision of the fund's authorising officer, is excluded. Because according to the provisions of the bill, the source of funding for the payment of the benefit will be among others. the loan from the Labour Fund, the provisions of the project provide for a different procedure than that of the Public Finance Act, for changes to the financial plan. In line with the provisions of the draft law, there will be an increase in the entitlements of the Labour Fund and an increase in commitments on the part of the Solidarity Fund for the Support of Persons with Disabilities, On the other hand, the Public Finance Act indicates that a change in the plan may take place during the year in case of an increase in over-planned revenues, while costs may increase if they do not increase the planned status of the fund's liabilities. Since the bill introduces additional tasks of the fund resulting in an increase in costs which will be financed by commitment, it is appropriate to exclude the provisions of the Public Finance Act in this respect. This procedure is also applied in other laws introducing additional tasks for funds, e.g. for the CSO, and results from the need to comply with legislation. So that most of the changes we are talking about are the result of the law, Mr President, and certainly the finance committee on which Members sit, there is no reason to give an opinion on the changes that the entire House will adopt by law. So much for this. As far as employment is concerned, I have mentioned a little bit how it is growing and, at the same time, how the number of people in the country at working age is actually falling. Of course, people with disabilities are also part of our society and they also have the right to retire, sir. As regards the question of Mr Wilczyński, how many people will not receive the benefit if they exceed the lowest pension for incapacity for work. Mr President, there are a lot of people here. However, since the law applies to persons unable to exist independently and some people will only apply for a judgment, we cannot say precisely. On the other hand, if you have had the chance to read the bill, you know that it also applies to the lowest pensions, the lowest pensions, social pensions, social assistance benefits and people who do not have any benefits at all, so of course you have a large number of social groups. Mr Pyzik asked if there would be 30 days for the ruling and referred to 30 days for the ruling by the district board on disability. Of course, we say that independence will be decided by the Social Insurance Institution juror, but we have stated that for people with significant disabilities there will be up to 6 months to get a ruling, and the benefit will be granted from the moment the application is submitted. Ms Ewa Tomaszewska asked whether income from work would not be included in this benefit. Of course, no income from work will be included because we want independent people, people with disabilities to be active. And, of course, the Act makes it clear that this applies to those declarations financed by public funds and is financed by public funds: pensions from the Social Insurance Institution, pensions from the Social Insurance Institution, social pensions which are also paid by the Social Insurance Institution, although they are financed by the budget, and benefits from social assistance, which are also financed by the budget, i.e. by public funds. And it's over. Everything else: income from work, income from renting rooms and income from capital loans, income from compensation does not include what we are talking about. Of course, this is very important information for people with disabilities. Mr Maciej Masłowski - what other information will be needed to obtain the decision? Mr President, you must actually ask the Social Insurance Institution, but I understand that the Social Insurance Institution actually, if it is in doubt, also uses medical records and various other documents. As far as the benefit is concerned, we know that you must be 18 years old, and at the same time the benefit should not exceed the lowest disability pension, so if someone has a high pension or pension, then of course the decision will have to be refused. On the other hand, the granting of the benefit will certainly take place through an administrative decision and, of course, from an administrative decision, e.g. The Social Insurance Institution serves an appeal, also to the social security court. And, of course, an independent court decides if there are doubts. Mrs Marczulajtis asked yesterday about the case law and I have indeed prepared myself. The decision on the disability group could be obtained by 1 January 1998. Until 1998, there were Invalidity and Employment Commissions, but their decisions are still valid and valid if they have been issued for a permanent period. There is no longer a Medical Committee on Invalidity and Employment at the Social Insurance Institution, and there is no I, II and III of the disability group, but those who have been included in one of them still remain in the light of the law of disabled persons and have retained their rights, unless the decision on their disability has lost or has been amended. The disability groups translate as follows: I disability group - total incapacity for work and independent existence, II group - total incapacity for work, III group - light disability, partial incapacity for work, etc. So if someone had an I disability group, they don't have to file a motion for a judgment. Its judgment corresponds to a judgment on total incapacity for work and self-existence and is sufficient to obtain a supplementary benefit. This proposal also changes the rules on tax liability. It is proposed to introduce a limit on the amount from which the obligation to settle the split payment mechanism will take place. It was assumed that this mechanism would be covered by goods and services documented by invoices of more than 15 000. zł or equals that amount. Draft Print Act No 3255 includes a change in the way goods and services are identified for VAT purposes and the departure from the application of the Polish Classification of Products and Services, PKWiU 2008, to the CN nomenclature for goods and the current PKWiU 2015 for services. The provisions of this project can be grouped into the following blocks: the first is the new VAT matrix, the second - changes in the flat-rate system for farmers, the third - changes in the reverse charge system and institutions of joint and several liability, the fourth - the introduction of binding joint information, in short the WIS, and the fifth - technical provisions, consisting of the transition from the current PKWiU 2008 to the corresponding CN symbols. The Public Finance Commission has adopted a resolution on joint consideration of the draft laws in print no. 3255 and 3602. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u337	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft Act amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact assessments and certain other laws, contained in print No 3616, entered the Sejm on 9 July 2019 and was subsequently addressed to the first reading in the Committee on Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry. The Commission at its meeting on 17 July in the first place in accordance with Article. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm have examined the application to proceed to the project within a period of less than a 7-day period, since 6 days have elapsed since the date of referral to the committee on 11 July. The proposal was adopted by 13 votes to none, with seven votes against and one abstention. At first reading, a request was first made to reject the project at first reading. A request for a public hearing was also submitted on 4 August. Both of these proposals were rejected by the committee in the vote: 8 votes in favour, 15 against, 0 abstentions. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The main objective of this law is to speed up proceedings and re-bureaucratise procedures related to environmental impact assessments of projects. Investors are looking forward to this, especially local authorities. The Government of Law and Justice has prepared - unprecedented in height - support for municipalities and powiats for the reconstruction of local roads of PLN 6 billion. The communities of cities, municipalities and powiats are pleased to finally have the renovation, modernisation and construction of new roads, which will create better conditions for business and commuting to work, schools and will make life easier for people, and the mayors, mayors and starosts are concerned that they will not be able to prepare project documentation for planned and so expected investments, because the extension of the assessment of the environmental impact of the planned projects makes it impossible to undertake further stages of the project work, prepare appropriate technical documentation, publish tenders and start the planned investments. The second, and no less important, objective of the draft law under discussion today in print 3616 is to ensure full transposition of Directive 2011/92/EU and to take account of at least some of the allegations arising from the letter of formal notice sent by the European Commission in March 2019 - that is, infringement 2019/2024 in the field of incorrect transposition - by introducing a new solution in Article. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 a law which will enable the Regional Environment Director to consult other competent authorities on the basis of the type of investment in case of cross-border environmental impact of a project carried out in another Member State of the European Union, e.g. the opinion of the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the environmental impact of the offshore pipeline. Secondly, it will allow clarification of the Article. Article 66 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point (1) (a) c Act by indicating that the description of the project should include an estimate of the expected residues and emissions and waste generated not only at the stage of operation of the project but also at the stage of its formation. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Since, at first reading, there were allegations in the debate of the project that it was incompatible with European Union law, I would like to stress with all my power that, according to the opinion of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the project is in line with European Union law. [[Interesting.]] In line with the timetable for the work submitted to the European Commission, it would be good for the Sejm to adopt the law in July this year. The issue is therefore particularly important. I am therefore very surprised, as rapporteur for this project, that, after rejecting the above-mentioned proposals, which were obviously to delay the work on the bill, Members of the opposition clubs did not work on the bill and were not even present, with the exception of two Members, at the committee meeting. High Izbo! The proposal amends three legal acts, i.e. Environmental Information and Protection Act, Construction Law Act and Environmental Law Act. It concerns three types of procedures related to environmental impact assessments, i.e. First, strategic environmental impact assessments, second, environmental impact assessments, and third, cross-border environmental impact assessments. And now, briefly, about the solutions proposed in the project. First, with regard to these strategic environmental impact assessments. Firstly, it will be necessary to remove the need to assess all local land-use plans and to leave such an obligation only to those plans which provide a framework for projects likely to have a significant impact on the environment. Secondly, it will be possible to derogate from the strategic evaluation for newly created local plans, not just for their changes, as it is now. The beneficiary of this new solution will be local authorities who, after 10 years of operation of the law, see that a mandatory assessment of each local plan is not necessary. Thirdly, it will be possible to eliminate the need for a strategic evaluation in the case of draft documents which are prepared solely for national defence or civil defence purposes, as well as financial or budgetary projects. These are the issues concerning strategic impact assessments. On the other hand, in the field of environmental impact assessments, the project provides, on the one hand, facilities for public administrations and, on the other hand, facilitations for entities planning to implement projects or preparing strategic documents which define the framework for the implementation of investments. An example of this is e.g. enable the investor to submit documents to request an environmental decision in electronic form or exempt it from the obligation to include extracts from land registers, which may be several dozen, even several hundred. Especially in the case of linear investments, such as roads or railway roads, this solution is not only a financial relief, but also shortens the time needed to obtain these documents. This time can sometimes be really long. Secondly, there will be a reduction in the Director-General's burden on environmental protection and regional environmental directors in order to speed up the adoption of environmental decisions for key infrastructure projects relevant to regional and national development. First, it will be the transfer of the Director-General's powers as referred to in Article. 76 Act, Regional Environment Director for the control of environmental decisions issued by local government units. In addition, there will be a transfer of the regional environmental director's competence to the authorities of local government units in matters in which the municipality is an investor and in matters relating to closed areas, but with the exception of closed areas established by the Minister for National Defence, as the projects carried out in these areas are of particular importance for defence and State security. Third, in the article. 74. 3a The law will change the concept of the area to which the project will affect and, consequently, confer the quality of the party to the proceedings, making these concepts independent of the division of the geodetic area, i.e. the boundaries of the registration parcels, which should not affect the substantive aspects of the impact of the investment. As you know, the impact here is associated with distance, which is working in favour of all the neighbours, because it is not enough to have a narrow strip of parcels adjacent to the investment, it is only said to be a distance from the plot that is subject to investment. Fourthly, the parties to the proceedings shall be notified to the notice in accordance with Article. 49 of the Code of Administrative Procedure, when their number is greater than 10, and not as it is now, when their number is greater than 20. Fifthly, in the article. 1 amendment of the 25th draft is added Article. 86d Act, according to which suspension of proceedings on the issue of environmental decisions will take place only at the request of the investor and not at the request of all parties. The point is that the suspension of proceedings is a procedural act, this action does not affect the substantive examination or settlement of the case, so this provision can be shaped. Sixth, there will be an elimination of the obligation not to carry out an environmental impact assessment. The body will find that there is no such obligation in the environmental decision, because we are currently dealing with some kind of duality, i.e. a decision in this regard both in the decision and in the decision. This is simply an unnecessary element of the procedure. Seven o'clock, in the article. 2 The Construction Law is amended to seal the investment process and to more transparently link environmental and construction rules by indicating directly the cases in which environmental decisions are required prior to the construction start, as well as clarifying the catalogue of activities that change the way the building is used or part of it. The change in the record removes the existing interpretation doubts and clearly indicates that a change in the way of use will be undertaken in the building site of such an activity, which is one of the projects requiring environmental decision. And a third group of issues related to cross-border environmental impact assessments. This is what the project envisages in this regard. First, in the amended Article. 108 The projector abolishes the obligation for the investor to translate documentation into official languages of all countries in which the implementation of the investment can affect the environment if it affects more than two countries. In practice, this means that the documentation can be translated into English. Unless Poland is e.g. related to a specific country to some bilateral agreement which gives rise to commitments in this respect. This will clearly translate into a reduction in expenditure related to translation of documents. Secondly, the project abolishes the obligation for an investor to translate documents which are not required in the light of the current rules of international law and the transfer of them to vulnerable parties has so far generated additional costs. Thirdly, the amendments to the translation of the necessary documentation have also been introduced in the provisions standardising cross-border proceedings for draft strategy papers. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! During the detailed work on the project, one substantive amendment was introduced concerning the amendment to Article 29. 1, concerning the retention of point 2 in Article. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4. The first version of this amendment was that points 2 and 3 are deleted. It is now noted that point 3, point 2 of that paragraph is deleted. 4 stays. This will make it necessary to translate the request for a decision for the affected country, as this request should be given in the official language of that country. This is what another provision of this law says, so if we removed point 2, there would be a contradiction in this case. This single substantive amendment has also been introduced in the text of the project. 31 amendments were also made by the Legislative Bureau, which were accepted by the government. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The whole project contained in the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry was adopted by 13 votes in favour, with 2 votes against and no abstentions. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law will significantly facilitate the process of environmental impact assessment procedures. It will be the legal act for which local authorities, entrepreneurs and many circles of our society are waiting, and ultimately local communities, who are waiting for all the necessary investments to improve their quality of life. Therefore, Mr Marshal, High Izbo, on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I ask you kindly to adopt the draft law contained in the committee's report in print No 3661. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u338	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jan Duda, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u339	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Act on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact prices and certain other laws. The draft law presented by the government in print No 3616 aims to simplify and streamline the procedures for preparing and issuing decisions relating to the impact of investments that are intended to be carried out by investors, on the environment and public participation in them. The solutions proposed in the draft law aim to reduce bureaucratic burdens and simplify legislation. The proposed changes concern those aspects of environmental decision-making which constitute real barriers to investors, but also significantly hamper the activities of state institutions, especially regional environmental directors. The removal of these barriers seems reasonable and does not jeopardise the achievement of environmental objectives, especially since the project also introduces important solutions to seal the investment process rules in the context of the powers of the architectural and construction authorities. The project is also in line with the objectives of sectoral documents, including, inter alia, with the objectives of optimising the administrative procedures set out in the 2020 strategy. The adoption of the Act will be particularly important in the face of investment in Poland which is crucial for the economic development of the country, especially in the field of road and rail infrastructure. The draft was discussed in very detail by the rapporteur. So I'm just going to narrow it down to those elements of the project that are of great interest, that is, the parties' participation in the proceedings and the definition of the party. The project, which must be clearly stated, extends the number of parties to proceedings when issuing environmental decisions, maintaining the status of the party, as enshrined in the amended Act, not only owners of parcels adjacent to the investment parcel, but also extends it to owners of neighbouring parcels within a radius of 100 m. And it's whether they're bordering on the investment or not. In addition, it gives the status of the parties to all those who are in the area of possible negative effects of the investment on their property if such effects are demonstrated. A clear definition of the party leads to realization and acceleration and a reduction in costs for public investment, the roads, railways and bridges I have already mentioned. It should also be made clear that the proposed project does not alter the status of organisations and associations as regards their participation in the decision-making process of administrative authorities, although expectations, in particular of municipalities, are clearly stated in order to organise this participation. The project also improves the process of issuing administrative decisions by municipalities in the case of public investments for which the municipality is an investor, while maintaining full regional control of the environmental director over compliance with nature conservation regulations, local law acts. Procedures for failure to carry out an environmental impact assessment shall be abolished. This will be stated once in the decision. A very important change in cross-border procedures is the abolition of the obligation for an investor to translate documentation into the official languages of all countries. If this applies to more than two countries, it will be English. This is in line with the ESPOO Convention. Such a solution will significantly reduce costs on the part of the investor. The explanatory memorandum attached to the draft provides an in-depth and convincing justification for each of the amendments made. For such a clear and clear justification, I very much thank the authors of the project - the Minister, the Director. The project at the meetings of the Environment Committee was well worked, the amendments of the Legislative Bureau and the whole were discussed and accepted - positively assessed by the Commission. High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, after reading the committee's report and accompanying it [[♪ The bell ♪]] draft, print No 3661, fully supports it and will vote in favour of its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This area means the envisaged area in which the project is to be carried out and the area located 100 m from the borders of the site. We keep reading because it's not over. This is a binding agreement for him - not an opinion, but an agreement that is binding on him. These are probably additional burdens for local authorities, and consequently they lead to a decisive acceleration in the resolution of many issues or proceedings. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u369	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour of acting today before the High House on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the revision of the Education Law. This law concerns the operation of military uniform classes. It is worth noting that we are not making any revolutionary changes here. This is simply a legal and organisational arrangement of the facts. The Act does not introduce any additional tasks for schools and local authorities, but sets out the statutory framework for the functioning of so-called. Military prep units, military uniform classes. In Poland today there are about 500 schools with such classes, while the training programs that are implemented in these classes, or the level of theoretical and practical classes are very diverse. In order to ensure uniformity of educational standards in these classes, both in practical and theoretical training, changes should be made to the Educational Law which will ensure standardization of teaching in these classes. Thanks to the novel, we will ensure uniform standards of military training in the so-called. military prep units. We also want - here I refer to Members of the applicants, I am also one of the Members of the applicants signed under this law - to ensure that these uniform training standards are established in cooperation with the Ministry of National Education. As a result, graduates who complete their training in these classes will be ready to undertake further military service, they will also be able to study in military universities, because those who complete their training in these classes will be preferred in getting into such studies in military universities, as well as those who complete their training in military classrooms, will be ready to perform a short-term preparatory service, take a military oath and fund the personnel reserves of our army. It is worth noting that the current solution, which is based on the fact that schools run military classes within the framework of the so-called. pedagogical innovation, which allows directors to shape any programme in these classes, for example, is detrimental to the defence of the state, is also detrimental to graduates of such classes, because today this training, which they hold in military classes, does not result in any specific benefits for them, for these graduates. It is worth noting that the project does not generate any new costs on the part of local governments. Until now, local governments, or bodies running schools, had to cover all the costs. This project changes that. On the side of the Ministry of National Education there will be an increase in educational subsidies to cover the labour costs of teachers in these classes. To date, these costs had to be fully covered by local authorities. At the same time, the Ministry of National Defence will cover the costs of practical activities that will take place in military units cooperating with schools conducting such classes, as well as the grant to the school authority for such activities. So far, in the pilot programme, the Ministry of National Defence has allocated about PLN 4.5 million to such classes. If the costs of running such classes are higher, the funding will be covered by the managing authority. In general, the specific costs will be set out in the Regulation. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act will help to strengthen the Armed Forces' personnel reserves, will also enable the Armed Forces to provide volunteers, as well as to strengthen defence education and patriotic attitudes. Therefore, on behalf of the club Law and Justice [[♪ The bell ♪]] We declare our support for this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u370	Please, Mrs Magdalena Ewa Marek, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u371	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Coalition Club, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending Education Law, print No 3548 and No 3671. Uniform, military, police, Border Guard and firefighter classes have been operating in Poland for many years thanks to bottom-up initiatives, thanks to the involvement of teachers, school heads, parents and students. Over 20 years, regulations have been introduced that have enabled and improved the functioning of uniform classes. The programmes of the Minister for National Defence and the Minister for Education have been organizing these processes and cooperation that have been going on for many years. In order to fully reflect the context of the proposed amendments, a number of provisions need to be mentioned. In 2000, for the first time, the concept of uniformed classes appeared. On 9 April 2002, a regulation was issued by the Minister for National Education and Sport on the conditions for negotiating and experimental activities by public schools and institutions which gave such an opportunity. On 9 June 2009, the Minister for National Defence issued Decision 187 on the implementation of the principles of cooperation between NGOs and the social partners. This enabled experts to cooperate with schools and to enable school classes to take place on the ground and in military units. Three years later, in 2012, a list of schools was established in the Ministry, which was extended to include further schools that introduced an extension of classes in terms of safety education. The formation of uniform classes coincided with the increase in social activity within the framework of pro-defence organisations. Today's 50,000. students learn in schools when it comes to uniformed classes. This shows how much interest young people have in uniform services. The planned amendment of the law is an attempt to formalise those classes which have been in the education system for years. The solutions contained in the project aim at the possibility of organising military preparations in secondary schools. Military education is to be carried out as an objective independent of education for security. The role of the Minister of National Defence is large. He will participate in the activities of these classes, in the creation, organization, but also in training in military units. The Act is due to enter into force on 1 January 2020. Recruitment is to take place in the spring, while from 1 September 2020 these classes are to be formally launched according to this amendment. It is also worth mentioning that a pilot programme has been in place since 2017. In 2017, the Ministry allocated almost PLN 3 million to it. In 2018 - PLN 4.5 million. The proposals that have been drawn up under this pilotage will be included in the Minister's Regulation. The project, however, has flaws. One of the drawbacks is that it introduces changes in the functioning of local government units, introduces new tasks for schools and bodies running these schools. There is such an iron rule that no new tasks cannot be introduced without additional funding. The minister at the committee meeting said that this funding would be provided, so we hold the Minister's word. I regret the fact that there was no opinion of the local government, and yet in the case of parliamentary projects such opinion should be. The second problem that is evident in this amendment, in the Education Law, is that of about 50,000. Unicycle students only learn part in military classes. For this project to be comprehensive so that this project does not need amendments should be made at this stage in a few months. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u407	The explanatory memorandum states that the military class concept emerged in 2000, while the first military class was organised in 2001. Despite cooperation with schools, the Ministry has so far not had a greater impact on the way in which classes are conducted in uniform classes. This draft law is intended to significantly change this. However, I would like to ask whether the Ministry has an analysis carried out on how the adoption of the law could increase the interest of the relevant individuals to create this type of class in the coming years.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u408	The draft deals with the introduction of provisions enabling the operation of military preparations troops to be legally determined, so-called. Military uniform classes. The concept of military classes appeared in 2000 and assumed to launch a pedagogical experiment which, after the testing phase, would end in the introduction of a system solution into the legal order. The first military class was organized in 2001 in Tuchola High School, acting according to the military program by colonel. dr. hab. Julius Tym. The conduct of military classes allowed the provisions of the non-binding regulation of the Minister for National Education and Sport of 9 April 2002 on the conditions for innovative and experimental activities by public schools and institutions (Journal of Laws No. U. poz. 506 and 2011 item 1051). In the absence of legal acts governing the establishment and operation of uniformed schools, they themselves lay down the main principles for the functioning of such classes in accordance with the provisions of the field of education law, resulting in a wide range of programming and organisational diversity. The experiment, which began in 2000, continues until today and participates in about 500 schools, which shows a positive perception of the concept of military classes in society. National Defence Resort has supported these innovations for years, but from a practical point of view, the solution is rather unfavourable if the State's defence performance criterion is taken into account. Despite intensive cooperation, the resort has no real impact on the way in which classes are conducted in uniform classes. In the light of existing law, the Ministry may offer a certain range of support (e.g. assistance in the implementation of classes, youth training programmes, teacher training), but must not set standards and enforce them. Experience shows that not all institutions carry out classes at a satisfactory level from the point of view of defence activities. Another problem is that there are too few hours at the disposal of teachers. Military education in the framework of innovation is mainly conducted during the hours available to the school head as an extension of the subject of education for safety, but this is not an extension within the meaning of the provisions of the Education Law because it does not involve a senior subject. We will be in favour of further work on the project.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u412	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, it is my honor to present our position towards the government's draft law amending the Fire Protection Act, print no. 3619. It is worth to say first of all that voluntary firefighters are the oldest social organizations, organizations with exceptional achievements, organizations operating throughout Poland. The first units of voluntary firefighters were established on Polish lands more than 150 years ago in Krakow, Kalisz, Raciborz or Brodnica. They responded to new threats that destroyed large areas of rapidly expanding cities in the 19th century. After Poland regained its independence in 1918, voluntary firefighters were created as legal entities, which was supported by the politics of the Second Republic. In 1921 the unions operating under the partitions merged into a single structure called the Chief Fire Management Board of the Republic of Poland. The structure of the volunteer guards has been so shaped to this day. It is worth noting and reminding that during World War II the Fire Resistance movement was created. This environment has great merits for Poland's independence. Currently in Poland in the so-called. the TSO system is registered with 16 170 TSOs. Of course, this number is fluid, because there are more units in Poland than 16 400 operating units. The legal basis for the activities of TSOs is: the Law on Associations, the Fire Protection Act and the statutes of entities registered in the National Court Register. At the head of each unit, the TSO shall be the President elected every 4 years with the members of the Management Board and the Head responsible for the rescue operations in the unit concerned. At this point I would like to point out that over 800,000 currently operate in the CSR lines in Poland. persons, including around 500,000. active firefighters involved in rescue operations. According to the Fire Protection Act, these are 18- to 65-year-olds. Volunteer firefighters in Polish reality conduct a rich educational, cultural and sports activity. There's 10,000 at the TSO. youth fire teams, 1630 female fire teams, 800 brass orchestras, 360 artistic teams, 3,200 sports teams, 1,400 memory rooms. 6,000. individuals carry out rich chronicles of their activities. Thousands of rescue operations carried out every few minutes throughout Poland are a basic form of CSR activity. For fires, firefighters leave for local events on average 520 thousand. times a year. This was the case in 2019 and these are both events and rescue actions. All these events are 50% attended by volunteer firefighters and 50% by volunteer firefighters. together with colleagues, officers from the State Fire Department. At this point I would like to point out that in 2019 voluntary firefighters operating under the national rescue system, and they are in this 4400 system, received funds of PLN 80 million and units outside the system - PLN 44 million. For the first time in the history of the units received support from the modernisation programme of PLN 501 million for the purchase of rescue equipment. This is due to the Government of Law and Justice. In 2018, the units received support from the Justice Fund amounting to PLN 102 million. Of course, they also receive support from the national environmental fund and from the voivodship environmental funds. In the bill submitted today in the Sejm, this year the CSR units will receive support of PLN 82 million in the open selection of applications for statutory activities. After the adoption of the Act and its publication, the units will be able to submit applications within a specified period, through the Provincial Commandant, to the Minister for the Interior. The appropriations obtained must be cleared by the end of the financial year. The proposal, the contract will specify time limits and rules [[♪ The bell ♪]] expenditure of these funds. Measures under the provisions proposed in the Act may be obtained for sports, cultural, educational activities...

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u470	Dear Marshal! Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Health Committee on the Government's draft law amending the law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of certain workers employed in medicinal entities, print No 3618. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 11 July 2019, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Health Commission for first reading. The draft law amending the Law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of certain staff employed in medicinal entities amends the amount used to calculate the lowest basic salary of staff of medicinal entities currently covered by the Act of 8 June 2017 on how to determine the lowest basic salary of certain staff employed in medicinal entities. The amendment concerns the calculation of the lowest basic salary for the period from 1 July 2019 to 30 June 2020. The aim of the project is to change the so-called height. the basic amount on the basis of which the lowest basic salary is calculated so that at 1 July 2019 it is increased from PLN 3900 gross to PLN 4200 gross. The proposed amendment of the Act of 8 June 2017 will not result in the transfer of additional financial resources from both the State budget and the National Health Fund to healthcare providers in 2019. In the case of workers covered by the draft amendment to the Act whose employers receive funds from the National Health Fund, the cost of increases should be financed by employers from the resources obtained under the increasing revenues and correspondingly the costs of the National Health Fund. The proposed regulation is not covered by European Union law. The proposed law will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. The Health Commission, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 17 July 2019, requests that the High Sejm adopt the draft Print Act No 3618 without amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u471	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u472	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the club Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position on the draft law amending the law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of some workers employed in medicinal entities, print No 3618. The draft law is one of the elements of a systemic solution aimed at gradually improving the pay conditions of medicinal workers and reducing inequalities in the level of basic salaries of different groups of medicinal workers. The aim of the bill is to increase the level of the lowest basic salaries by changing the so-called. the basic amount on the basis of which the lowest basic remuneration is calculated. This amount at 1 July 2019 was PLN 3900 and is to be increased to PLN 4200 gross according to the project. The amendment concerns the calculation of the lowest basic salary for the period from 1 July 2019 to 30 June 2020. In accordance with the provisions of the past law as at 1 July 2019, the basic salary of a medical professional and a basic worker other than a medical professional shall be increased by at least 20% of the amount which is the difference between the lowest basic salary and the basic salary of that worker. This amendment is intended to accelerate the pace of raising basic salaries and the achievement by the target workers of the lowest basic salaries set out in the Act of 8 June 2017. The draft law does not set the remuneration of certain staff members of medicinal entities, but merely sets out the lowest legally permissible basic salary for such staff. The draft envisages a change in the deadline for the signing of an agreement between employers and trade union organisations. There is a provision giving a monthly deadline for the signing of an agreement which will be the basis for these amendments. In the absence of an agreement between the employer and the trade union organisations, the employer has 14 days to implement these changes by order. On the basis of this project, workers employed in medicinal entities will be able to obtain several hundred PLN increases in the basic wage. I would like to stress that this project is part of a sustainable strategy for raising the salaries of medical personnel, and this is because the situation in the country, economic development, low unemployment, the increase in average wages in the economy leads to an increase in revenues from the National Health Fund and on the basis of this Prime Minister, the Minister could decide to increase the basic amount from PLN 3900 to PLN 4200, without waiting for the end of 2020. I would like to remind you that the National Health Fund's financial plan has already changed several times. With regard to the original plan and to the entities, namely hospitals, an amount of 7.5 billion more than the original plan was intended. Therefore, it appears that there should be no turbulence associated with the implementation of this law. The proposed changes, I hope, will have a positive impact on the situation of workers covered by this regulation whose basic salaries are lower than the lowest basic salaries in the wage net. Following the proposed change in wages, they will need to be increased, which seems very good not only for workers but also for health professionals and, above all, for patients. For years, we have been struggling with a small number of medical personnel and it seems that all the changes in wage increases that Law and Justice has been making in recent years are already delivering the expected results, for example in the area of nurse employment. We know that, for example. This year, for the first time, all graduates of medical schools received the right to pursue their profession. So you can see that this trend is changing in a positive way, and I would like to remind you, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that in 2015 only 30% took away the right to pursue the profession.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u503	Thank you very much. And I would like to ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Health, Minister Józef Szczurek-Żelazko, for an answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u504	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! As already noted here, this law entered into force two years ago. Two years ago, we discussed in very detail why this law was created, what is the purpose of this law. The same discussion also took place a year ago when we amended this law. We have explained to you what the purpose of this law is, why such regulation is taken by the government. Now, like boomerang, the same questions are coming back, so let me remind you again. In 2015, when we analysed the level of remuneration of individual employees within the various occupational groups as well as within one group, examining the remuneration of employees on comparable jobs, it turned out that there were huge differences in the remuneration of individual employees. Chimneys were created, and this level of wages was far removed from e.g. comparable salaries at the same workstations in the same therapeutic entity or other medicinal entities. Many times to the Ministry of Health, government representatives have just been influenced by the fact that there is a huge inequality in terms of employees' salaries. Therefore, the Government, the Government of Law and Justice, decided to prepare a project and then to re-act and implement a law that regulates the minimum level of basic remuneration. This law was the first act of this type, which was prepared and implemented by the current government. We accepted it in order to establish a minimum remuneration at a level that would guarantee a good level of remuneration. We have taken into account various factors or criteria in the drafting of this law. This project was strongly discussed in the Social Dialogue Council, in the joint government and local government committee, as well as being the subject of public consultation for at least 30 days. They all had the opportunity to make their comments. And that was the purpose of this law: to compensate for inequalities and to indicate a minimum level of remuneration. We were aware that many of the workers who had been educated in our country: doctors, nurses, diaphragms, physiotherapists, unable to count on a decent salary, decided to leave the country, and unfortunately began to become more and more, year by year felt a lack of medical staff. This law was therefore intended to prevent such situations. I will now turn to the answers to specific questions. The honourable Member asks why the base amount would be raised to PLN 4200. I explained it to you on the committee, but let me explain it to you again. Ladies and gentlemen! By adopting the bill 2 years ago, we thought that the base amount of PLN 3200 would be the amount that would give a real rise to the salaries of employees, but on the other hand, the directors of medicinal entities would be able to raise it to that amount, taking into account the amounts provided by the National Health Fund for the provision of health services. At that time, 2 years ago we decided that this base amount was safe at the level of PLN 3200. Following an analysis of the collection of contributions, the country's economic situation, the current government, the Minister of Health, came to the conclusion that it is possible to increase this basic amount to PLN 4200 and thus to increase this ceiling of increases which will take place since the middle of this year. This amount was discussed within the Social Dialogue Council. It is not true that it was not discussed, because it was the Social Dialogue Council that was expecting an increase in the basic amount at the moment. The representatives of the Social Dialogue Council considered that we should really still discuss indicators, but here and now it is important to raise the base amount. And this was negotiated, we got an agreement, which is why this amount was introduced into the bill. The increase, of course, has been in force since 1 July, not since 1 January, as you said. The draft of this law did not introduce the date of 1 January, always the date from which these increases would be made was 1 July. Many of the questions raised the question of covering the consequences of this regulation. I have explained this to you many times in the Committee on Health, and we very often present it in public space: there is a progressive increase in health expenditure. The measures shall be transferred to the therapeutic entities. This year, as you said, there has been a four-fold change in the financial plan, the cost of health benefits has been increased and lump sums have been secured. I would like to remind you that from 1 April only the amount of PLN 680 million was allocated to the increase in the value of the benefits, which included an increase in the value of the benefits... [[Billions short.]] I'm only talking about benefits that are performed in the surgical and internist ward. Only in these two branches did we raise this amount. As we have calculated, it turns out that these amounts should already be enough to finance the cost of this increase. Another revision of the financial plan was made in June and since 1 July there are further increases in the valuation of benefits, as well as an increase in the lump sum, among others. for hospital rescue units of 5 and 15% , also in the field of rehabilitation, sanatorium treatment. It's all included, among others. in the increase in the financial plan. We estimate that increases in rehabilitation and sanatorium benefits will increase by around 5%. So it is not true that there is no increase in lump sums. There is a clear increase in lump sums. This year we are already planning that we will spend over PLN 97 billion on health protection, which is an obvious increase, for example, compared to 2015, when 76 billion PLN was spent on health protection. We estimate that there will be another increase in resources this year. That's another line. Of course, she also raised the point that there would be a pay gap, that there would be no guarantee of raising the wages of the least earners, which is not true, because the least earners will benefit most from this law, because it forces even increases to this minimum level. [[What are the forks?]] In such a way that every year in the middle of the year each director is required to increase the basic salary - this year by 20%, next year by 20%, so this is exactly set out in the bill. Read it to yourself. The next question, Mr Kozłowski, was whether this law regulates wage problems comprehensively. You said it was ad hoc. This is not ad hoc because, as you can see, since 2017, the Act clearly indicates that an annual amount must be allocated to an increase in salaries. The Act sets out the final remuneration rates to be achieved in 2021. So this is not an ad hoc action, it is a system action which is aimed at guaranteeing an increase in the remuneration of all professional groups that participate in the provision of benefits. You also mentioned that physiotherapists and diagnes are unfairly treated. I don't know which record you read it from, because when we look at the table, that's where the education criterion is. Thus, if the diagnosis has a higher education and specialization, it has the same rate and level of remuneration as a representative of another profession with a higher education and specialization. So there is absolutely no discrimination here. If a physiotherapist or a diagnostician has a bachelor's degree, this is also comparable, the same minimum remuneration as in the case of a representative of another profession with this level of education. So that's what eliminates unfair treatment of individual workers and these disparities. Mr Łopa asked what we had solved by this law, whether the decision to increase wages should be made at the level of managers. Yeah, she should, but she doesn't. The Minister regulates this law because it simply doesn't collapse. That's our answer. You also asked about the costs. I've talked about these costs many times. Four-time revision of the financial plan, the appropriations are earmarked for this. If we increase the value of benefits, then in health care there are labour costs. So... [[Is there real debt?]] ...if there are labour costs, then there is automatically money for wages. This is equivalent to the fact that, by increasing the financing of the benefit, we are transferring funds to increase wages. This is the case and there have already been several increases. [[Is there a real amount of debt?]] The debt problem, Mr President, must be addressed in a number of contexts. [[Real?]] I think that the issues of management skills and supervision should also be looked at. So that I think these are other issues that determine these effects. Another question, Mr Ruciński, was also a lump sum. You also mentioned that flat rates don't grow. Dear Member - there is no Member - if you do not believe what we say, please address the Regional Directors of the National Health Fund and request data on the lump sum for the previous year and this year of the specific medicinal entity. You will see that there is a clear difference. I stress: flat rates from year to year, from month to month, are increasing. I already told you about the base amount. The question was why not change the coefficients, but the base amount. Because it was established in the discussions, in negotiations with the social side, at the Social Dialogue Council, where we talked about this for hours. The main expectation there today was an increase in the base amount, hence the government's decision to prepare a project that would increase the base amount. The Member also said that this increase in the basic amount could become a norm. Yes, it will become the norm because that is exactly what we want to increase this base amount gradually, depending on the situation in the national economy. That's what we're trying to do, because as a result of the base amount, the salary will increase. Of course, since 2020, this basic amount will no longer be set out in the Act, but will be linked to the average national remuneration in the national economy. Mr Skutecki also mentioned, saying that it was a nice thing that money was flowing to doctors. So once again, I'd like to point out that it's not just the doctors. This is a law whose title itself speaks about shaping the remuneration of employees employed in medicinal entities. It's the law that's the least affected by doctors at this point. So I think it's worth noticing, too. Next question: Will the queues be cut short? This is a complex problem. You said that even if we put the other one in there, the queues wouldn't be cut short. Dear Member, from the moment we have clearly increased the salaries of nurses and doctors, we have eliminated the phenomenon of travel, labour migration. As you said here, at the moment doctors do not go abroad. Not enough, they come back and employ themselves in the Polish health care system. [[Then listen to the nurses.]] The proof of this is the fact that last year [[♪ The bell ♪]] more professional rights have been issued than nurses have completed their studies in the nursing departments.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u535	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for these questions. I hope I can answer them precisely enough. I begin with questions by Mr Skutecki and Mr Masłowski about assistants. This is the case: with regard to the number of assistants who present these electronic exemptions today, we unfortunately do not have such data. The Social Insurance Institution has them because today these are data, these are documents, these are e-ZLAs that are processing the Social Security Office. That is why we do not have such information, but the Social Security Office will certainly be able to give you such information and we will know what the number is. With regard to Mr Masłowski's question as to whether such an assistant can serve, support more than one doctor, yes, it is possible here: many to many, i.e. each doctor can have more than one assistant, and each assistant can support more than one doctor. With regard to Mr Sachaika's question, which is where to get medical assistants, this is, of course, an option that everyone can benefit from. We know that in many medical subjects these assistants are already functioning today. The idea of introducing medical assistants in practice came actually at the time of the introduction of electronic layoffs, mainly because it was indicated that already very often one person writes paper releases and then gives them to the doctor only for signature. We found that if we were able to create a reliable IT system that would handle it, then we could do it in such a way that someone already on behalf of doctors on the basis of medical records, where the period of incapacity for work is indicated, would be able to issue such release, save it into the system. That's why the idea came up. We give everyone freedom. Therefore, we also offer the possibility to finance this from the sources available to the healthcare provider. We don't influence it here, there's no obligation to hire assistants. We just want to make it possible and sort of sanitize what's already working in the health care organization. As far as this shift is concerned, indeed - I am actually fighting my chest here - we are making this shift, Mr, by 7 days, from 1 January to 8 January. This is due to my personal traumatic experiences this year when I spent New Year's Eve at work and we were waiting for this first stage, this implementation that started on January 1. This is, as far as I'm concerned, my team, our responsibilities. I'm saying a little joke, but we know that this is also the work of hundreds of hospital computer scientists and medical professionals who would have to de facto come to work on January 1st, and you all know that we all have completely different things to do on January 1st. That's why such a shift seems rational. So that January 1st, later the Feast of the Three Kings, then one working day and then another day, on which you can move with these changes. It seems like such a practical, rational move. As for the questions that Mr Papke has asked when there is a full system, ladies and gentlemen, this system is actually being created now. The first element, the e-recipe, is already functioning. As far as e-direction is concerned, piloting is ongoing. As far as electronic medical records are concerned, she's on her way, and that's something that's gonna come up next year. However, it is worth pointing out that we are also making such a big change here in the context of this vertical data flow, i.e. we are introducing the possibility of making the data available under the law to the primary healthcare doctor who has this patient on his list. This is very easy because such a doctor always - we want to strengthen the role of family doctors - will have by law, without the need for additional consent, access to information about prescriptions, referrals and this electronic medical documentation. With regard to the date of entry into force of the obligation to keep this electronic medical file, to allow it to be exchanged, this is, ladies and gentlemen, 1 January or really after the changes we want to propose here, 8 January 2021, which is the year after the entry into force of the e-recept. As regards Mrs Czernow's question, which is the deadline for completing the prescription, we are actually extending it in the case of an e-prescription, because the situation with respect to the ordinary prescription is slightly changing, because the e-prescription can be partially realized, i.e. it will be possible to rewrite the dose for 360 days, while it will be possible to implement the individual parts of this e-prescription without having to obtain any copies. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u569	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government's draft law amending the Insolvency Law and certain other laws in print No 3480. The Sejm at its 82nd meeting on 12 June 2019 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. The bill includes e.g.: the possibility of declaring consumer bankruptcy in a simplified manner without the need to appoint a Judge Commissioner, the possibility for debtors to enter into agreements with creditors under the direction of a restructuring adviser or the authorisation of an Assistant Judge to act in civil proceedings. As a general rule, the project aims to simplify the procedure for declaring consumer bankruptcy and the burden on insolvency courts. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights after considering the draft law at its meetings on 14 June and 16 July this year. asks the High Sejm to adopt the law without amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u570	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Kazimierz Smolinski, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u571	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I am pleased to present the position of the club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the bankruptcy law and certain other laws, print No 3480. The solutions proposed in the draft respond to the weaknesses of the current consumer bankruptcy model. The main instrument to better address the problem of over-indebtedness is to introduce the possibility of declaring bankruptcy of a non-business operator in a simplified procedure, i.e. without the appointment of a Judge Commissioner. This solution is intended to significantly remove the burden of handling the simplest cases of consumer bankruptcy from the judicial administration, which is currently effectively blocking the efficiency of bankruptcy and restructuring courts. This solution is directed primarily at cases where, in the course of bankruptcy, there is a lack of significant assets to satisfy creditors through the sale and execution of the distribution plan, or where disputes concerning the amount of debt are not expected to arise. In addition, the project introduces the possibility for debtors to enter into an arrangement with creditors with limited to the minimum participation of the court under the direction of a licensed restructuring adviser who will at the same time ensure that the agreement is implemented. It is assumed that this solution will significantly reduce the increase in new proceedings where it is necessary to declare the debtor bankrupt. Just like in the case of... I'm sorry. Another instrument provided for by the Act to improve the insolvency procedure is the failure of the court to examine the debtor's culpability in order to deepen the insolvency situation, as in the case of insolvency of businesses, the insolvency situation should first be decided, and not the assessment of the debtor's culpability in bringing the debtor to or deepening it. The existing solutions create significant differences in the case law of the various district courts and contribute to the emergence of insolvency tourism within the country. Any inappropriate behaviour of the debtor that is not a crime or act directly directed at the victimisation of creditors will be examined at the stage of establishing the creditor's repayment plan, so when the debtor's behaviour can be claimed by the creditors. Then the decision will be made. The project also clarifies that for a non-business debtor it will be possible to propose the rapid sale of all assets subject to execution in the form of the so-called liquidation. pre-pack, which increases the flexibility available to the debtor for quick exit options from insolvency and new life start. The new regulation is complemented by the adoption of a solution whereby the waiver of liabilities without a repayment plan will be conditional in all cases where the inability to make any repayment under the creditor's repayment plan is due to a personal insolvency situation which will not be permanent. This is intended to prevent excessive default without any repayment to creditors, thereby encouraging debtors to undertake a repayment plan, which in principle is expected to last a maximum of 3 years for the insolvency option and 5 years for the conclusion of an agreement with creditors. In addition, the principle that the declaration of claims should be submitted directly to the restructuring adviser acting as a syndicate at the place of the system in force is introduced. In addition, the following amendments to the laws are proposed: Code of civil procedure - civil proceedings in addition to the referee can also be carried out by the Judge's assistant; Law on Court Costs; Law on the License of Restructuring Advisor, where the obligation for restructuring advisors to create a file is introduced; Law on the Assistance of persons entitled to maintenance - amendment in order to clarify the doubts arising in practice regarding the application of the Act in the field of insolvency proceedings against the maintenance debtor; Restructuring Law, where changes of an adjustment nature have been required; National Debt Register - also adjustment provisions. The draft also provides for a time-limit according to which these proposed provisions on the consequences of initiating proceedings on the person, assets and liabilities of the failing party will also apply to legal events which took place before the entry into force of these provisions. In order to ensure certainty as to the application of the law, the rule of application of those three provisions was adopted in cases in which an application for bankruptcy was filed before the date of entry into force of the law, with the exception of those laid down in Article. 1 and Article 1 Article 2 and Article 2 5. The Law and Justice Club, given that the project was very strongly consulted and found strong support from the social side, supports the proposed solution and will vote for it in its entirety. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u572	Thank you, sir. Mrs Zofia Czernow, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u573	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition club on the government draft law amending bankruptcy law and some other laws, print No 3480. The proposal to amend the Insolvency Law and certain other laws aims at simplifying the insolvency procedure for consumer insolvency. The PO-PSL government introduced new comprehensive solutions for consumer bankruptcy in 2014, and since then there has been a breakthrough in the extension of Poles. The entry into force on 1 January 2015 of the new rules on consumer bankruptcy has significantly changed the legal situation of insolvent natural persons not engaged in business. New, better conditions have been created for declaring consumer bankruptcy, resulting in a rapid increase in the number of applications in this case. In 2015, more than 5600 applications for consumer bankruptcy were received by the court, which represents a nearly twenty-fold increase compared to 2014. When a year later, i.e. Since 1 January 2016, the scope of consumer bankruptcy has been significantly extended to former entrepreneurs during the year since the removal from the relevant register or cessation of business activity, the impact of applications has been avalanche. The consequence of this regulation is a significant burden on the courts and an extension of the time taken to deal with cases. Therefore, the simplification changes are now necessary. The submission proposes a number of further facilitations in the procedure, including inter alia. introducing the possibility of declaring bankruptcy of a non-economic person in a simplified procedure, introducing the possibility for debtors to enter into agreements with creditors with limited participation of the court, giving up the need for the court to examine at the stage of declaring bankruptcy the debtor's culpability in bringing the debtor into debt or deepening that condition, allowing the debtor not to sell quickly all the assets subject to execution in the form of liquidation, filing claims directly to the restructuring adviser, establishing that the waiver of liabilities without a repayment plan will be conditional. During the legislative work of the Emergency Sub-Committee and then in the committee, the participants raised a number of doubts and even reservations which could be the result of the solutions adopted. First of all, the need to distinguish small-scale entrepreneurs from those who operate to a much greater extent and additional criteria were introduced into the project. The Commission has ultimately not decided to introduce a split-up of entrepreneurs, but I believe that this issue needs constant monitoring. For it may turn out that this equalisation of entrepreneurs can lead to the practical use of this situation. The most important discussion was on the article. 491 and detailed provisions on the possibility of determining the reasons for insolvency: due to fairness, purposefulness, gross negligence. There have been indications of serious difficulties in proving the intended actions which may, as a result, be detrimental to creditors. All these doubts require an ongoing assessment of the effects of the solutions adopted and the response to possible irregularities. Today, however, it is premature to assume that legislation will be used in bad faith. Parliamentary Club Civic Platform - Civic Coalition will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u576	Thank you very much, sir. Mr Michał Jan Mazowiecki, Polish People's Party - Coalition Poland.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u577	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club Polish People's Party - Coalition Poland I have the honour to present a position towards the government draft law amending the bankruptcy law and certain other laws. The aim of the law is to improve the new model of consumer bankruptcy in Poland since the end of 2014. This amendment led to a significant increase in applications for consumer bankruptcy and bankruptcy proceedings. In 2014, only 32 of these bankruptcies were declared, while in 2018 there were 6552. It is worth noting that the number of such proceedings is increasing systematically, even after the introduction of a new model at the end of 2014. Between 2015 and 2018, the number of cases has increased more than twice and the number of bankruptcies declared has tripled. These data show that the regulations introduced in 2014 filled a significant gap in the Polish legal system. Unfortunately, a significant proportion of Polish adults experience difficulties in the timely regulation of commitments. As at the end of 2018, the number of non-regulated persons was 2.78 million. This applies to sums of at least PLN 200 against one creditor who has been delayed by at least 30 days. The number of debtors and the amount of outstanding liabilities is increasing despite economic prosperity and the increase in social transfers. The consequence of the rules introduced a few years ago is also a much higher burden on courts to declare consumer bankruptcy. Across the country, there are only 30 economic departments of regional courts that serve consumer bankruptcy. This means that in 2018, an average of 424 affected cases fell into one department. The investigation of 240 randomly selected cases from nine centres, carried out by the Institute of Justice, showed, inter alia, that insolvency proceedings had lasted too long. The consumer remains subject to the extension procedure usually for about one year and is then covered by the repayment plan for a further 36 months. The greatest benefit of the proposed solutions is to simplify procedures and reduce the burden on courts of consumer bankruptcy. The adoption in 2014 of the new model of consumer bankruptcy filled a significant gap in the Polish legal system, but at the same time led to an avalanche increase in cases resolved by the economic departments of regional courts in such cases. As a result, the burden on these departments has increased significantly. The solutions proposed in the draft, in particular the possibility of declaring bankruptcy in certain cases in a simplified procedure and the possibility for debtors to enter into agreements with creditors under the direction of licensed restructuring advisers, should lead to a reduction of this problem. The cost of the proposed solutions is likely to reduce the chance of creditors to meet their claims in some cases. The draft law simplifies the procedure for obtaining consumer bankruptcy and increases its availability for new groups of debtors. A significant cost of the proposed solutions may be to reduce the sense of liability for debts incurred resulting from the possibility of obtaining a decision on consumer insolvency of persons who have led to their insolvency or have significantly increased its degree intentionally or because of gross negligence. Such regulation can lead to abuse, intentional debt by some citizens and then to bankruptcy. This regulation can also encourage new groups of people to seek consumer bankruptcy and thus increase the burden on courts of this type. Despite some shortcomings of the revised version, after taking into account all its good points, the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club - Coalition Poland [[♪ The bell ♪]] is in favour of this bill. Thank you very much.

The Council of Dialogue with the Young Generation is to deal with the opinion of cases concerning young Poles, as well as cases concerning draft legal acts and government programmes concerning the young generation. The amended act is to enter into force 14 days after the date of its announcement. The Council of the first term of office is to be appointed within 6 months of the entry into force of the Act.
