ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u613	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 38 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the draft law amending the consular law (prints 3615 and 3685). I ask Mr Józef Leśniak to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u614	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the draft law amending the consular law, in print no. 3615 and 3685. The amendment of the consular law is primarily of a technical nature. Its aim is to improve the functioning of the existing law of 25 June 2015. consular law. I would like to stress that this is not a political act, although it is introduced during the election or pre-election period. The aim of the amendment is to improve the work of the Polish consular service. The decision to amend the consular law was taken after extensive consultation with the consular community, which clearly defined what was lacking in this law and what should be amended in order to improve work in this area. The scope of the amendment covers four main issues. Firstly, these are issues related to the passing of the consular examination. The possibility of withdrawing from the examination is introduced in the case of candidates for consular officials who have successfully submitted such an examination during the 12 months preceding the date of appointment for office and candidates who have ceased to perform consular functions in the same period in the previous establishment. Secondly, it is the issue of exemption from pre-travel training for particularly experienced consular officials if their knowledge, professional experience and practical skills guarantee the proper exercise of consular functions. Thirdly, it is a question of the procedure for setting up examination committees and the possibility for persons who are members of the foreign service to enter the examination committee, who do not have a diplomatic degree, but whose knowledge, professional experience and skills predispose them to such a role. Finally, fourthly, these are issues related to the amendments to the consular law, which stem from the experience of daily consular practice and eliminate the problems encountered by our consular service. It goes on, among other things. ensure the interaction of administrations, courts and institutions in the country with consuls by ensuring access to information or documents. The catalogue of actions which are understood by the concept of consular assistance shall also be extended. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The Committee on Foreign Affairs, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 18 July 2019 and following the introduction of legislative and editorial amendments, adopted without objection a report requesting the adoption by the High Sejm of the attached draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u615	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mr Krzysztof Ostrowski, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u616	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the parliamentary draft law amending the consular law, print no. 3615. The proposal aims to amend the consular law of 25 June 2015. In general, it aims to simplify the functioning of the consular service. The most important proposed changes, as the previous speaker said, the rapporteur, are essentially concerned with four areas: consular examination, training, examination commission and consular assistance. The other amendments are of a corrective and more precise nature to the current rules. As regards the area of the consular examination, the proposed regulation provides for a derogation from the need to pass the consular examination in two cases. The first case shall be exempt from the obligation to pass the consular examination when the candidate for consular officer in the last 12 months prior to the date of designation for consular duties has successfully submitted a consular examination for prior designation as consular officer. The second case is a candidate who, within a period of not more than 12 months before the date of appointment as a consular officer, has ceased to perform consular functions, who has been released in connection with the earlier exercise of that function. This solution will be effective in terms of flexible management of staff within the corps of consular officials. Training service is the second area. The person exempt from the obligation to take the consular examination will also be exempted from participation in pre-trial training and will, as a general rule, participate in pre-election training. It is envisaged that the Director-General of the Foreign Service may exempt a candidate from pre-accession training as a consular officer. Area three is the examination board. The new law introduces the possibility of setting up a consular examination panel for persons who are members of a foreign service who do not have a diplomatic degree when their knowledge, professional experience and skills predispose them to such a role. The last area, from such major things, is the area of consular assistance. In order to improve the effectiveness of consular assistance, it is necessary to ensure that the consul cooperates with courts, prosecutors and other public institutions in the country, in particular by ensuring access to information or documents. The lack of legal basis for such actions has led to little effectiveness. The draft law also extends the catalogue of actions which are understood as consular assistance. Polish citizen will be able to apply for financial assistance. Aid may be granted to him where a third person makes the payment of the amount applied for by a Polish citizen, or if the applicant Polish citizen declares that the amount of financial assistance granted to him will be reimbursed within the time limit specified in the decision to be given by the consul by providing him with financial assistance. The Act will enter into force within 2 months of its publication. It is in line with European Union law. Ladies and gentlemen! Mrs. Marshal! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will fully support the proposed law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

It clarifies de facto and complements the consular law that has been in force for 3 years. It shall indicate and clarify the areas in which consuls carry out consular activities, establish an open catalogue of matters which may be dealt with by consuls and consular officers authorised by them. Within the framework of consular assistance, the consul will be able to transfer a fixed amount to a Polish citizen in a situation requiring consular assistance if a third party pays that amount to secure his basic needs, to the bank account of the consular office, diplomatic representation or to the office serving the minister responsible for foreign affairs. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u632	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice with regard to Draft Print Act No 3657 on ratification with prior agreement expressed in the Act of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the United States of America on strengthening cooperation in the field of preventing and combating serious crime, signed in Washington on 12 June 2019. The essential effect of the entry into force of the Agreement will be to strengthen security and public order protection in the field of preventing and combating crime. The Agreement establishes a legal framework for cooperation between the competent authorities of both Contracting Parties by exchanging information concerning inter alia. the relevant circumstances relating to the offence and the techniques and methods of action of criminal organisations. Through mutual access to databases containing fingerprint data, the agreement will be a tool for rapid and effective cooperation. The actions taken will aim to reduce the number of serious crimes, among others. against life and health, human trafficking and smuggling of migrants and illicit trafficking and trafficking of narcotic drugs, precursors and psychotropic substances. As regards the possible economic impact of the entry into force of this Agreement, it is currently difficult to foresee, but given that this instrument should contribute to the reduction of crime, it should be assumed that these effects will be positive, in particular because the Contracting Parties undertake to prevent and combat crimes against economic turnover: fraud, corruption, the laundering of proceeds from crime. The scope of the agreement is also covered by cooperation aimed at combating counterfeiting and counterfeiting, counterfeiting and trafficking of goods, counterfeiting and the marketing of documents. In view of the above, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Law and Justice we declare our support for this initiative, we will vote in favour of it, as we ask other clubs and parliamentary circles as well. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u644	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to thank you very much for this discussion, for this debate. To answer the questions that were raised during this discussion, I would like to say that the point of contact is the Police. Here it is clearly defined, clearly defined. The contractual provisions also clarify the scope of the protection of personal data, how these personal data are to be processed, how they are made available, etc. First of all, I pay attention to the article. 6 contracts which indicate that the transfer of further personal data and other information which is available for fingerprint data is subject to the internal law of the requested party. As far as personal data protection matters are concerned, there is also Article. Ten deals. I'll quote two sentences. In the mouth. 1 We have information that the parties recognise that the processing of personal data which they receive through mutual exchange is extremely important to ensure confidentiality in the implementation of this Agreement. And an important mouth. 2: The Parties undertake to process personal data in a fair and consistent manner in accordance with their internal law. If we're talking about our country, we have these internal rules, they apply. We have a law on the protection of personal data processed in connection with the prevention and fight against crime. It is the Law of 14 December 2018 and it is as effective as possible in this respect. And one more suggestion, one more remark. This was also highlighted by the Members in the committee's meeting, and here also this thread was moving. It's about art. 11, which clearly confirms that the parties do not provide data obtained under this Agreement to any third country, international organisation and private entities without the consent of the data exporter and without adequate safeguards. So that matters relating to the protection of personal data are secured in this agreement, which, as rightly put forward at the outset by your rapporteur, was drawn up in Washington on 12 June 2019. One more job. The conclusion of an agreement on the prevention and fight against serious crime, I would like to make it clear, is one of the conditions necessary for Poland's integration into the American visa-free movement programme. In this context, the agreement is close to the standard of such agreements concluded by the United States with other countries. We have a number of countries that have already signed a very similar agreement and have already ratified it. We are talking about the Federal Republic of Germany, we are talking about the Italian Republic, we are talking about Spain, we are talking about Portugal. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u678	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Today I am pleased to present a draft law amending certain laws to reduce regulatory burdens. But before I do, maybe two sentences of such a short introduction. Legislative work in our ministry can normally be classified as one of two categories. Some of our proposals are of such a nature, I would say, a systemic change in some area of law. Yes, here the most flagship example is certainly a package of laws called the constitution for business. Another such example could be, for example. Succession. But we also have, let's say, a second category of legislative work, and these are deregulation laws, package laws, which improve the legal environment in which entrepreneurs operate in a broad sense. And these laws tend to be a little bit of a character, if I may say, a patchwork. They are also the result of such a properly ongoing dialogue with entrepreneurs. As part of this dialogue, we collect requests, proposals, ideas, and once in a while we just group them together in one act. The second category includes this project, which I have the pleasure of presenting today. It contains more than 50 specific point changes in 66 laws, in the broad sense of economic law. I don't think I have to convince anyone how important this task is to reduce the regulatory burden for entrepreneurs. This is particularly important for SMEs, because there is a lot of research that shows that the smaller the business, the smaller the business, the more regulatory burden they cost, because such businesses often cannot afford, say, specialist assistance and lawyers or tax advisors. All the more so from the point of view of the competitiveness of the SME sector, trying to improve the legal environment, reducing burdens is particularly important. I do now. Since there are a lot of these concrete proposals, I will focus on some of the most important ones. I think... I'd like to start with what we call the password right to make a mistake. Sometimes I also often allow myself to call it the right to a yellow card. This is a solution that already works in legal systems, e.g. France. That's where we got them. And the idea is this. The idea is that entrepreneurs who are natural persons during the first year of their business activity, i.e. start-ups, can benefit from the right to error without being punished by administrative penalties, which would normally involve violating this provision and will only receive instruction. What's important, and I would also like to make it clear, that doesn't mean, you can say, something like that, that nothing is happening, because the condition of exercising the right to error is of course to correct such an offence as well. Of course, it cannot be serious, heavy, especially heavy. However, for individuals and for beginners, such a more friendly approach appears to be justified. Another proposal also involves, a little by little speaking, the friendship of economic law or the legal environment for entrepreneurs. This is to make it possible for businesses, including entrepreneurs, who, as in the previous proposal, are natural persons, or to put it this way: entrepreneurs operating on the basis of registration with CEIDG. Now, on this proposal, I would first of all like to make this point. Consumer law is, to a large extent, a European law based on directives and these directives allow the possibility, not from now on, only for a long time, to extend consumer protection, which, of course, normally belongs to consumers, but to operators in the SME sector. We take advantage of this possibility and extend it to situations where an entrepreneur, a natural person enters into an agreement with another entrepreneur, although directly related to the business activity carried out by it, but not having a professional character for it. It seems to me that this solution is also justified, that it breaks up with such a fiction of professionalism. Because very often they talk about entrepreneurs, about all entrepreneurs together, en block that they are professionals. And that, of course, is, on a daily basis, justified. However, of course it is not so that if someone e.g. he's, I don't know, a locksmith or a businessman in his company, e.g. entertaining or consulting, he is also a professional subject when, for example, comes to him, I don't know, buy yourself a printer for a company where he prints something, true that he knows how to buy printers to the extent that he knows about his particular area of activity. And it seems to me that in terms of consumer protection it is worth taking advantage of and it is worth to break with - I would call it - a fiction of the professionalism of entrepreneurs, individuals, i.e. those registered in CEIDG. Another change in this spirit, although more, let's say, limited subjective scope, is the change that enables crafts to be carried out in the form of a company. We in Poland have more than 200,000. people who wear crafts. I feel like this is a group of entrepreneurs that you sometimes forget about. She is very important, what's more, I think some market trends suggest whether they allow you to hope that it's a craft, if I may say so, will come back. As you can see, there are trends that emphasize that people are increasingly choosing custom-made items that are somehow characterized by mass production. I really hope it will be an opportunity for Polish craftsmen. I am also aware that the possibility of doing crafts, doing business in the form of a company will not be attractive as if for most, for all, but it is a necessary development path for these companies, these craftsmen, who, I say, are doing well, they are doing well, they are developing, they are developing. Entering the start-up stage of the company is kind of a natural development path. Today they do not have it, because it would in fact mean that there is no possibility of using professional qualifications, referring to these. I would also like to stress that this proposal is also very strongly supported by the craft industry. We also have changes that I would define in these successive stages, successive stages related to the success of companies. There was a law, which I myself mentioned earlier, of such a more systemic importance, just addressed to entrepreneurs, individuals, to entrepreneurs from CEIDG. This is a point solution which also allows the transfer of administrative decisions relating to the running of a business, where the trader in life, I stress, sells them or transfers them free of charge to his successor. This is very important, because first of all, as far as succession is concerned... For completely natural reasons, this is an increasingly important problem in Poland, because this is the first generation after the breakthrough, and entrepreneurs are often at such a age that they think about transferring the company further. It is important to stress very strongly that this process of success does not always involve the death of entrepreneurs, but it is very often still going on in the life of this entrepreneur, who would like to gradually withdraw from the company or would like to pass it on. This is a provision that allows such a transfer of decisions, which means someone has some licenses, permits, may carry them in their lifetime, even though they were previously issued specifically to this person, this older entrepreneur. This is a solution that will significantly facilitate the succession in life. The latest solution of the larger ones, which I would like to mention even from this point of view, is a tax solution, but it is actually of some broader economic importance. It concerns the settlement of VAT on goods and services, VAT. Why do I say it matters more? In fact, I should start a little bit different about this change. We are in a situation where our ports compete very strongly with ports primarily German, Dutch. We have data from the National Tax Administration, which shows that - given the value of imports - about 30% of imports that go to Poland do not go through Polish ports, but mainly through ports such as Hamburg and Rotterdam. One of the very important, not the only one, of course, but of the very important, factors determining the competitiveness of ports is how VAT is accounted for in imports. In Poland it is currently 10 days from customs clearance, in these ports west of us it is on general terms, i.e. the 25th day of the following month. This may seem a small difference, but when you are, as we have been, in contact with this import, logistics industry, it is desirable as one of the important factors determining the competitiveness of ports. So it is a change, as I say, seemingly small, linked to the deadline for VAT settlement in imports, but in fact aimed primarily at improving the competitiveness of Polish ports vis-à-vis those ports with which they most often compete. We are simply counting on the fact that at least part of this turnover, which anyway goes to Poland, will go to Poland not by Hamburg or Rotterdam, but by Gdansk or Szczecin, which will, of course, relate primarily to the development of these ports, but also worth mentioning that it will also involve a duty, which will then be paid in Poland. As I said, there are more changes, I allowed myself to mention only the most important ones. The expected effect of these changes is - in general, this common denominator for the project - the reduction of administrative burdens, some such facilitation, making the legal environment more friendly. For this reason, I ask you very much from this point of view to accept the project and direct it to further work. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u679	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Wojciech Murdzek, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u680	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present to you the position of the Government's draft law amending certain laws in order to reduce the regulatory burden on printing no. 3622. The draft law, also called the "Friendly State" package, is a cross-cutting and deregulation project aimed at implementing further facilitations for entrepreneurs. She wears, among other things. improving part of the specific administrative procedures, increasing the re-use of data held by public administrations, repealing unnecessary information obligations, clarifying ambiguous rules, limiting certain types of excessive reporting and adapting the rules to technological developments. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, when preparing this proposal, the Council of Ministers was motivated by the need to simplify the law, eliminate inconsistencies and ambiguity of the law and maintain only the necessary regulations. Such an action was planned in the Responsible Development Strategy. In addition to such profound changes as the introduction of the so-called. the right to error, the extension of consumer protection to natural persons engaged in business activities, further facilitation of business succession and changes to the VAT Act and trade law, the government proposes to introduce a number of other point changes which are also needed. These include the elimination of the tax levy for entry in the register of postal operators and the register of qualified trust service providers, the digitisation of certain reporting obligations for liquid fuels or the reduction of the number of information submitted per year for the provision of payment services. Particular attention should be paid to the introduction of a silent settlement and simplified procedure in specific administrative procedures. This will speed up and simplify the procedure, but will also improve and reduce the costs of the functioning of the public administration. The silent settlement of the case is that, where there is no decision of the authority after the deadline for the settlement of the case has expired, the matter shall be deemed to have been dealt with in full taking into account the request of the parties. There is no need for a decision. There are two modes of silent handling. The first one is a silent termination of proceedings. It is based on the fact that the matter is deemed to have been dealt with in accordance with the request of the party where the authority has not delivered a decision or order terminating the proceedings. This procedure mobilises the Authority to deal with matters in a timely manner and counters the length of proceedings. The second mode is silent consent. The matter shall be deemed to have been dealt with in accordance with the request if, within the time limit laid down for the handling of the matter, the authority does not object by decision. The aim of the simplified procedure is to reduce the time taken to deal with matters by a simple nature which do not require full investigation, based on a certain action plan. They are characterized by speed and limited formalism. It is only possible to settle the matter in simplified proceedings if there is a clear basis in substantive law. It should be stressed that this proposal provides for such provisions and will allow this model to be applied in specific cases, for example in the areas of public roads, maritime areas and maritime administration, Water law. The solutions envisaged in the project are necessary to ensure better conditions for doing business and by removing the various administrative barriers to unlock the potential needed for further business development in Poland. The project will therefore have a positive impact on the situation of all players in the market and is certainly a step in the direction that the economy wants. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The draft law limiting burdensome and excessive legal obligations for entrepreneurs deserves support. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club [[♪ The bell ♪]] favours further proceding this draft law and will support the proposed amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 61 of the agenda: Report from the Commission for Energy and the Treasury on the Government's draft law amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act and certain other laws (prints No 3656 and No 3690). I would very much like to ask Mr Dariusz Kubiak to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u694	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, I have the honour to present a report to the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Renewable Energy Sources Act and certain other laws, print No. 3656. The Commission for Energy and Treasury examined the draft law at its meeting on 18 July 2019 and adopted the draft with six amendments. The aim of this project is to implement additional actions to achieve the target of a 15% share of energy from renewable sources in gross final energy consumption in 2020. In particular, auctions for renewable energy sources, which will be carried out in 2019 under the transitional provisions of a project setting out the quantity and value of electricity from RES sold under auction, will be a means to achieve this objective. Ladies and gentlemen! As we know from the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber of 2018, if we had failed to achieve this level of 15% the share of renewable energy, we would have a serious problem. Poland would face the need for a statistical transfer of energy from RES from the Member States of the European Union, which would have a surplus of such energy. As the Supreme Audit Chamber points out in this report, the cost of this transfer could be up to PLN 8 billion. The aim of the proposed solutions is also to increase energy security, which should result in sustainable access to energy to final customers in the long term, while keeping energy prices as low as possible. Among the key changes introduced by the Renewable Energy Act are the extension of the prosumer support mechanism to business entities and the implementation of new mechanisms for the promotion and development of energy cooperatives in rural or urban-rural areas. The aim of this project is among others.: extension of mandatory time limits for connection contracts for existing RES projects, implementation of selected issues of the RES Act, resulting in the elimination of uncertainty on the part of entrepreneurs participating in the auction system and registered in the system of certificates of origin. In addition, the project provides for strengthening the credibility of Polish guarantees of origin, recognition of them on the basis of reciprocity, among others. in other Member States of the European Union and to increase the maximum capacity of biogas installations which will be able to benefit from FIP support in order to ensure predictable conditions for the development of stable energy sources, which at the same time bring additional benefits to the domestic economy in addition to electricity production. The project has also taken into account the risk of non-starting sales in the auction system due to the accumulation of concession procedures and ensures that as many projects as possible are implemented to achieve the renewable energy targets set for 2020. The project also sets out precisely the dates of the first introduction of electricity into the grid under the support scheme and therefore sets out precisely the level of RES charges in a given calendar year, as well as the rules under the procedure for the admission to auction of energy-producing installations prior to the date of publication of the auction due to the resulting interpretative doubts. The proposed changes meet the social expectations and expectations of micro- and small entrepreneurs and represent important solutions, in particular for RES power generators and the whole RES industry, equipment manufacturers, designers, installers and funders of these investments. I've dealt with it myself. Yesterday, for example, from my city, the energy center of Poland, Bełchatów, I got a lot of signals and voices worried about whether we could adopt this bill. Yeah, we're gonna make it, and I think a lot of people in the industry are gonna love this decision. I will also mention here that, with regard to the six amendments I mentioned at the beginning, one of them is an amendment aimed at clarifying the definition of agricultural biogas and facilitating the use of the various raw materials that arise at the processing stage of agricultural products. This is the use of very valuable raw materials for biogas production, which not only contain no impurities, but are also safe for the health and life of humans and animals. This will also have an impact on the improvement of the water and sewage economy. One of these amendments will also have an impact on the development of hydropower plants with a capacity of up to 2.5 MW through a support system, i.e. the possibility of benefiting from a guarantee premium. Here we are dealing with an extension of the power range from the current level of 1 MW to 2.5 MW. This will, of course, increase the number of units that produce energy, and thus, as far as energy from RES is concerned, this share will definitely increase. The Commission has also adopted legislative, editorial and clarification amendments. On behalf of the committee, I request that the Sejm adopt the bill submitted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-18-sejm-84-2.u715	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In 2001, the local authorities of Świdnica, headed by the town of Świdnica, took the initiative to build a ring road for the town of Świdnica. At the initiative and for the money of local governments, the concept of this ring road was developed. The average daily traffic of vehicles on a national road exceeds the average for the national road network. The dynamics of traffic growth are very high. The section of the national road No 35 between Wałbrzych and Wroclaw is one of the most heavy traffic in Lower Silesia. Tragic road accidents, which have been taking place on this road for many years within the limits of Świdnica, show that concerns about the safety of the inhabitants are very real. The safety of pedestrian and circular traffic is the most important reason for which the ring road should be built. It is also necessary to relieve the historic centre of Świdnica, to bring heavy transit traffic beyond built-up areas and to reduce the nuisance of more than 700 historical objects entered in the register of monuments, including the Church of Peace included in the UNESCO World Heritage List or the historic cathedral, which was included in the list of historical monuments. Designed national road bypass no. 35 is to take over about 75% transit currently carried out through the centre of Świdnica. The Commission's assessment of investment projects has clearly confirmed the need for urgent construction of the ring road. The complete concept of the ring road location was made. The project obtained the necessary environmental permits and was fully prepared to announce the tender for the technical documentation. Literally a few weeks to announce the tender. By decision of the Minister of Transport Sławomir Nowak the work was stopped, regional funds of the General Directorate of National Roads and Motorways were apparently needed by Warsaw. The road situation in Świdnica for the next several years has been further destroyed. Unfortunately, no efforts resulted in a return to this interrupted bypass project. Thousands of people's signatures, experts' opinions didn't help. The project did not find its place in the national road construction plan prepared by the previous government. I would like to draw attention to the need to take action to ensure that this necessary project for the construction of the Świdnica ring road is included in the National Road Construction Programme. Maybe it's worth to start by allocating funds to the Świdnica ring road documentation on national road 35. In Lower Silesia it has been known for years that it is necessary to rebuild the national road No. 8 on the section Wroclaw - Kłodzko. The National Road Construction Programme 2014-2023 (with a view to 2025) sets out investment priorities for the development of national road infrastructure to be implemented by 2025. The issue of improving the safety of traffic on the national road No 8, as well as the improvement of the important connection with Wrocław to the southern part of the Lower Silesian voivodship, has been addressed with particular attention in the work on the final shape of the above mentioned. the document, which was reflected in the task of rebuilding the section of the national road No 8 between Wroclaw and Kłodzek. Implementation of this investment is necessary in order to increase the capacity and speed of travel and eliminate dangerous places. In addition, international transit and transit of heavy goods vehicles outside the urban and rural areas would be carried out. The necessary substantive scope of the project should include the reconstruction of approx. 75 km of the national road No 8 to Section 2 x 2 together with the construction of bypasses in the village and reconstruction of crossings with other roads. The work currently being carried out in relation to the development of documentation should determine the final, most optimal cross-section of the road. The stage of implementation of the documentation shall have secured funding under State budget appropriations. I would like to appeal for the provision of adequate resources for the realization of this very important task. Thank you very much.
